# StatsCapybara — Powerhouse Roadmap

**Vision:** the site every statistics student gets sent to — and actually enjoys. Not just "learn a t-test," but the *entire journey of a research student*: design the study → collect and clean the data → run the right analysis → report it properly → do it all ethically. Every step interactive, correct, free, and calm.

This file is the master plan. The copy-paste prompts that execute it live in **[PROMPTS.md](PROMPTS.md)** — run them one per Claude Code session, roughly in the recommended order below. Both files live at the repo root, committed alongside CLAUDE.md, so they sync to every machine via GitHub Desktop.

---

| | At kickoff | Now (Jul 2026, post-P27) | Powerhouse |

| Courses | 4 (Stats 1–4) | 9 (Methods, Data, Ethics, ML, Writing all complete) | **9** in two tracks |
| Lessons | 45 | 95 | **~95** |
| Tool pages | 8 | 16 (+ power calc, APA formatter, datasets, flashcards, which-chart, planner, correlation, progress dashboard) | **~16** |
| Long-form guides | 0 | 0 | 4 cornerstone guides |

**Two tracks on the homepage:**

- **The Statistics Core** — Stats 1–4 (exists, complete).
- **The Research Toolkit** (new) — everything around the statistics:
  - **Methods** · *Research Design* — 12 lessons (questions → designs → sampling → replication crisis)
  - **Data** · *From Raw to Ready* — 10 lessons (tidy data → cleaning → reshaping → privacy)
  - **Ethics** · *Responsible Research* — 8 lessons (consent → QRPs → AI ethics → fraud & self-correction)
  - **ML & AI** · *Machine Learning for Researchers* — 12 lessons (train/test → trees → clustering → LLMs)
  - **Writing** · *Reporting Your Research* — 8 lessons (IMRaD → APA numbers → figures → discussion)

**Suggested course accents** (verify visually in both themes before committing; violet `#8b5cf6` stays reserved for the checks block, orange `#f97316` for highlights): Methods amber `#f59e0b` · Data cyan `#06b6d4` · Ethics slate `#64748b` · ML purple `#a855f7` (shift toward fuchsia `#d946ef` if it reads too close to Stats-1 indigo) · Writing lime `#84cc16`.

## Guiding principles (non-negotiable)

1. **No build step, no frameworks, no external dependencies.** Plain HTML/CSS/JS + the two generated/injected assets (`search-index.js`, FAQ blocks). Everything in CLAUDE.md stays true.
2. **Statistics must be exact.** Every quantitative claim in prose, FAQ, or interactive is verified against published values (G*Power, tables, `node -e` cross-checks) before it ships.
3. **Frozen-noise interactives.** Sliders transform the *same* data; only explicit "New sample" buttons reseed. Round-trip test every slider.
4. **Calm, kind, capybara-tasteful.** No ads, ever — Ko-fi in the footer only. No dark patterns, no imposed quizzes, no red-alert UI. Serious topics (ethics history) get a serious tone.
5. **Each lesson stands alone** and is fully integrated: curriculum entry, checks, FAQs, quip, glossary, quiz question, snippet/software where applicable, sitemap, search index, per-page SEO.
6. **One prompt = one session = one commit pushed to `main`.** The session commits its own work and pushes it to `main` — Hakan does not merge by hand. See CLAUDE.md's "Finishing a session" rule for the exact gate conditions and the one case where a branch push is the correct outcome instead.

## Phases 10–11 addendum (11 Jul 2026) — make it feel human

The build-out is done (P1–P36, with P37 waiting on real Search-Console data). The remaining risk isn't a missing feature — it's that the site *reads* like what it is: 95 lessons written by one AI in two weeks. The prose is accurate and warm, but its constructions repeat with machine regularity, and a reader who spots the pattern discounts everything else, statistics included. Measured on 11 Jul 2026 as the Phase-10 baseline:

- **Em-dashes:** median lesson page carries **24**, the worst **49** (min 13); `tools/faq_data.py` holds **272** more. A human editor uses a handful per page.
- **The self-answered verdict opener:** FAQ answers and prose beginning **"No — " / "Yes — "** ≈ **80×** sitewide.
- **The contrast punch** ("isn't X — it's Y" / "it's not about X — it's Y"): **21×**.
- **Stock moves:** "Notice how/that" 17× · "Here's the/why" 17× · "quietly" 37× · "The point is" 9× · "Think of it as", "the good news", rule-of-three lists everywhere.
- **Templated openers:** nearly every lesson's first paragraph rides the same em-dash-contrast rhythm ("Most of the pain in data analysis isn't the statistics — it's the shape of the spreadsheet…").
- **One meta-description formula:** 40 of 120 descriptions are built on "…do X **and watch** Y…".

Phase 10 (P40–P46) fixes this the way everything else on this site got fixed: a written standard (**VOICE.md**), a zero-dependency measuring stick (**`tools/prose-lint.js`**, so progress is a number, not a vibe), then six bounded editing passes that de-template the prose **without touching a single verified statistic, link, or interactive**. The goal is not to sand the personality off — quips, capybaras, and the playful chrome stay — it's variance: pages that read like they were written by the same person on different days, not by the same process in one pass.

Phase 11 (P47–P48) is the product-side counterpart: an interaction-feel pass (anchor-scroll offsets, canvas cursor affordances, unified copy-button feedback, animated `<details>` — the small tactile stuff that separates hand-made from generated), then the instructor play (a For Instructors page, `?embed=1` lesson embeds for slides/LMS, one-click PNG export of any viz) — because professors are the multiplier audience the human checklist already targets.

Phase 12 (P49–P57) is a batch of hand-picked fixes and upgrades from real use (Hakan's 16 Jul 2026 review of the live site): a run of lesson-interactive bugs and control tweaks across Stats 1–4 (P49–P52), two dead toolkit interactives repaired (P53), and four higher-blast-radius items — making in-prose links actually look clickable everywhere (P54), giving the Statistics Toolbox the prominence its growth earned and un-collapsing it on the homepage (P55), turning the flat "Why it matters" box into a branded "Capybara says" moment (P56), and a new specialized lesson on fitting psychometric functions / the PSE for psychophysics supervision (P57). These are independent and cherry-pickable; the two site-wide front-of-house items (P54, P55) have the highest impact-per-effort. **Each prompt in PROMPTS.md carries a recommended model and effort** — model: **Powerful** (Claude Sonnet, for well-scoped single-area work) vs **Extra Powerful** (Claude Opus, for site-wide / new-lesson / judgment-heavy work); effort: **High** (one focused pass) · **Extra** (elevated reasoning; multiple changes or light diagnosis) · **Max** (maximum single-agent reasoning + verification; high blast radius) · **Ultra** (multi-agent deployment — parallel build + independent verification/review).

Phase 13 (P58–P60) is the second requests round (Hakan's 17 Jul 2026 review). Two teaching-content upgrades: a **Signal Detection Theory** lesson appended to Stats 4 (the natural sibling of P57's psychometric functions, and the theory underneath the ML track's ROC lesson — the two must agree on AUC = Φ(d′/√2)), and a rebuild of Stats 2.6 so each non-parametric test gets its **logic explained and its own tabbed mini-playground** instead of a name-drop. Plus the front-door follow-through P55 started: **front door v2** — the nav reorganized around the homepage's own structure (Statistics Core / Research Toolkit / Statistics Toolbox, each a hover menu listing courses/grouped tools; the Curriculum and About tabs retired), the Toolbox dropdown grouped exactly like the toolbox page, the resume banner promoted into the hero with real presence, and the persona cards retired. Same model/effort legend as Phase 12. The two lesson prompts share registries (`curriculum.js`, `faq_data.py`, the search index) — run them one at a time.

## Phase 14 addendum (18 Jul 2026) — depth & reach

P1–P60 built the site out, made it sound human, and rebuilt the front door. A full-site review on 18 Jul found the growth surface has moved to three quieter axes:

- **Reach.** There is no course-level URL: `statscapybara.com/stats-1/` is a 404, the nav's course rows drop visitors into lesson 1.1 with no orientation, and there is nothing to put on a syllabus that says "this course" rather than "this lesson". Nine landing pages fix the 404s, give every course a shareable front door with its own OG card and Course JSON-LD, and print as a one-page syllabus (P61).
- **Depth.** For an exam-driven audience the assessment layer is thin: the checks, the quiz (~128 questions, instant-feedback only) and the flashcards all test *recognition*. Nothing on the site makes a student produce a number by hand and then shows the full worked path — the thing exams actually demand. Exam mode plus a deeper bank (P62) and a two-stage worked-problems library with instructor print-without-solutions handouts (P63–P64) close that.
- **Hardening.** `audit.js` checks the site's wiring exhaustively but not one line of its math — the core promise ("statistics must be exact") has no regression test — and external links were only hand-checked in quarterly P38 runs. **P66 (done)** shipped both guard scripts: `tools/math-check.js` (218 assertions on `viz.js`, under a second, a required gate whenever the math moves) and `tools/extlinks.js` (the P38 link leg, never a commit gate). The math passed clean on its first run — the site's statistics were already right; they're now held that way. **P65 (done)** covered the finger-first pass: all seven drag canvases declare `touch-action` (the suspected `ml/classification-metrics` offender turned out to have set it from JS since P15, so nothing actually scrolled mid-drag — it was normalised into the markup and given the missing `pointercancel`), grab targets widen to a finger pad on coarse pointers via `VIZ.grabRadius`, and no page scrolls sideways at 360px.

Plus the instructor multiplier P48 opened: preset lesson states so an embed can carry its exact configuration. **P67 (done)** shipped `SC.preset(map)` — an opt-in helper wired on eleven flagship lessons, with a `{ el, scale }` applier form so a URL speaks the units the lesson *prints* (`?z=1.96`, `?r=0.3`) while the helper snaps and clamps to the control's own step/min/max, and `teachers.html` gained a preset-carrying embed snippet plus the params table. Verified against the strongest available oracle: for all eleven lessons the preset state is **byte-identical to setting the same controls by hand**, under `?embed=1` and under the subpath server alike, with the round-trip law intact and every malformed param inert. Next: a small trust round — a feedback link in the footer, PWA shortcuts, section share-links (P68). Same model/effort legend as Phase 12. **P61, P67 and P68 all edit `site.js` — run them one at a time; P64 needs P63.** Everything else is independent.

## Phase 15 addendum (21 Jul 2026) — footing

P61–P68 landed (plus an ad-hoc curriculum reorder). A fresh full review found **no feature gap left worth building** — all three gates pass clean (audit 0 errors, math-check 218/218, prose-lint --strict 0) and the remaining work is footing, not surface:

- **Honesty.** GA4 runs on every page and is disclosed nowhere; `teachers.html`'s twitter:description still promises "no tracking". A calm privacy page, a quieter GA posture where it costs nothing, a sitewide claims sweep, and a README rewrite (the public repo still says 51 lessons / 5 courses) — **P69**.
- **Signal.** The recurring loops decide what to refresh with page views as their only input — no event says whether exam mode, PNG export, embeds, or the presets are ever used. A small, anonymous, taste-checked GA event set makes P37/P39 data-driven — **P70**.
- **Search.** Exact substring matching in a field where students type "hetroscedasticity". Typo tolerance, glossary instant answers, kind zero-result states — **P71**.
- **The keystone.** Every stop of "the entire journey of a research student" exists; no page walks the whole journey once. The fifth guide carries one shipped dataset from research question to APA paragraph to limitations, linking every course and tool where a student would actually reach for it — **P72**.

After P72 the roadmap IS the loops — P37 (still waiting on a GSC export), P38 quarterly (**its next run should sweep the P61–P68 surfaces**: landing pages, exam mode, the problems print modes, share-links, presets), P39 refresh — plus the human-only checklist, which is now the actual growth engine: distribution, not construction. One big build option deliberately NOT prompted: a **Turkish mirror** — real reach, but it roughly doubles a ~130-page maintenance surface; that's a strategic call for Hakan, not a session.

## Phase 16 addendum (15 Aug 2026) — de-AI, round two

Phase 10 was declared done on its own numbers, and a 15 Aug 2026 re-measurement shows both things at once: **every hard budget passes** (the banned constructions all read 0 today) **and the site still carries the fingerprint** — Hakan's verdict prompting this phase is that readers, students especially, can still tell. What the measurement found:

- **The em-dash budget became a target.** 730 in page prose, lesson median 6, and six pages sit at *exactly* the ≤10 cap. The short tool pages are the worst per word — `descriptives.html` 34/1k words, `effect-sizes.html` 33/1k, `power.html` 18/1k — where a human technical editor runs 1–3.
- **~1,070 more em-dashes on surfaces that never had a budget:** FAQ answers 126, `checks.js` 203, `glossary-data.js` 127, `software.js` 66, the inline-script strings (lesson verdicts, quiz explanations) 521. Reader-visible sitewide: **~1,800**.
- **Shape tells the budgets can't see:** 403 bold-lead bullets (`<li><strong>Term:</strong> …`) across lessons and guides, plus the rule-of-three cadence the soft rules name but nothing measures.
- **Vocabulary is already clean** — delve 0, "worth noting" 0, "keep in mind" 0, crucial 7 — and the two words a stock AI-word list would flag hardest, *leverage* (54×) and *robust* (42×), are statistics vocabulary here. Round two therefore adds **no word inventory** (rule 12's polarity lesson: measure in context, prefer shape rules to word lists).
- **The loudest tell is not prose.** `statscapybara.com/CLAUDE.md`, `/PROMPTS.md`, `/ROADMAP.md`, `/VOICE.md`, `/AGENTS.md` and `/README.md` are served verbatim (branch deploy + `.nojekyll`) and narrate the whole AI process; `tools/` ships the checkers' narration the same way.

The fix is **P73–P78**: P73 tightens VOICE.md + prose-lint (page ≤ 4 dashes *and* ≤ 8/1k words, FAQ trio ≤ 1, first-ever budgets for the five JS surfaces, semicolon/ellipsis watch so the tell isn't traded) and adds two measure-first metrics (bold-lead share, cross-page duplicate sentences); P74 (Core, 59 lessons), P75 (Toolkit 38 + guides + posters) and P76 (tool pages + homepage + root prose) pay the debt down with varied punctuation and reshaped lists; P77 does the injected JS layer and closes sitewide `--strict` green — the phase's exit number is **reader-visible em-dashes ~1,800 → under ~500, lesson median ≤ 2**; P78 moves Pages to a filtered Actions deploy so the planning docs 404 on the domain, with the honest limits (the public repo and its history) stated plainly for Hakan to decide. One guardrail over the whole phase: **de-AI is style and surface, never a claim** — nothing may assert or imply human authorship; the About section and `privacy.html` stay exactly as honest as they are.

## Phase 17 addendum (6 Sep 2026) — teaching alongside Stats 1

**Source:** the two lecture decks for the 2026/27 Stats 1 block (Weeks 1–4, 207 slides; Weeks 5–7, 109 slides) and the course syllabus (Moore, McCabe & Craig, *Introduction to the Practice of Statistics*, 10th ed., chapters 1–9, in the order 1 → 3 → 4 → 5 → 6 → 7 → 8 → 9 → 2; SPSS 29 with five mandatory seminars; a midterm in week 5 on weeks 1–3 and a final in week 9 on everything; **both exams on paper, with the university's formula sheet, printed statistical tables and a basic calculator**). The syllabus already sends students to a named set of site pages every week (its "Online practice" column), so the site is part of the course's delivery now. The question this phase answers is two-sided: where does a student who follows the lectures land on a page that speaks a different dialect, and what does a lecturer want to put on a slide?

### What the comparison found

Verified against the lesson prose (scripts stripped), not the sidebar titles:

1. **Exam dialect vs software dialect.** Three conventions the exam uses that the site does not. *Quartiles:* the textbook takes the median of each half of the sorted data; `descriptives.html` interpolates R's type 7 and SPSS's Frequencies default is the weighted average. On the lecture's 18-value dataset those give Q1/Q3 of **26 / 39**, **26.25 / 38.25** and **26 / 40.75** — three answers to one exam question, and a student who checks their hand work against the site's calculator concludes they got it wrong. *Two-sample df:* the exam's hand rule is the conservative **min(n₁ − 1, n₂ − 1)**; the site says Welch and never names the hand rule. *σ known:* chapter 6 introduces inference with a known σ (the z-interval x̄ ± z*·σ/√n and the one-sample z-test), and the site skips that rung entirely — §1.11 goes straight to t*, and §1.12's explorer takes a z as input and never derives one from μ₀, σ, n and x̄.
2. **Paper tables.** The exam is Table A (area to the left of z), Table D (t criticals by df and confidence level, z* along the foot) and Table F (χ² criticals). `tables.html` is a calculator. Nothing on the site trains the row-and-column lookup, the three moves (left = read; right = 1 − read, or the symmetry read of −z; between = larger − smaller), or reading a p-value as "between two columns".
3. **A whole lecture with no home.** Week 2 is probability rules (the general addition rule, independence and the multiplication rule, conditional probability and the general multiplication rule, Bayes with the law of total probability), de Méré's bets, the birthday problem, the prosecutor's fallacy, and the mean and variance of a discrete random variable. §1.7 has the disjoint-only rules, one conditional-probability paragraph and a Bayes FAQ; §1.8 opens on the binomial's four conditions. Zero prose hits sitewide for *disjoint*, *law of total probability*, *birthday*, *prosecutor*, *expected value* in the discrete-random-variable sense.
4. **Week 4 concepts with zero prose hits:** choosing one-tailed vs two-tailed from the question's wording, statistical vs practical significance, "absence of evidence is not evidence of absence", and β and power worked from a picture of two sampling distributions. §1.12 lists Type I and II in two bullets and sends power to Stats 3, which the syllabus marks optional.
5. **Chapter 7 details missing from §1.13–1.14:** the name *standard error of the mean*, the one-sample t confidence interval, the robustness rule of thumb (n < 15 / 15–40 / > 40), the two-sample CI, the "compare two one-sample CIs" mistake, Gosset.
6. **Chapter 9:** the lecture's worked example is a 3 × 4 table computed cell by cell (expected count, component, sum → χ² = 19.86, df = 6, p ≈ .003); the site's explorer is 2 × 2 only and shows no components, and the textbook's conditions (all expected ≥ 1, no more than 20% below 5) are not stated in that form.
7. **Chapter 2:** r as the mean of standardized products, b₁ = r·sᵧ/sₓ and b₀ = ȳ − b₁x̄, the line through (x̄, ȳ), the residual-plot patterns, scatterplot scaling, and the two class exercises every intro lecture runs (guess the r; a categorical variable hiding two positive trends inside one negative one). The playground exists; the hand formulas do not.
8. **SPSS.** Five mandatory seminars, and `software.js` has entries for **6 of 18** Stats 1 lessons — none for the descriptives / charts / z-score weeks that the first seminars cover.
9. **Exam prep.** Exam mode scopes by whole course; the midterm is §1.1–1.12. `problems.html` has 12 Stats 1 problems and none in the shapes the lectures work by hand: σ-known z procedures, probability rules, Bayes, a discrete random variable's mean and SD, power from a picture, an r × c χ², r and the regression line from a small table.
10. **Teaching surface.** Presets exist on 7 of 18 Stats 1 lessons; no lesson or tool can carry **data** in its URL, so the lecture's own numbers cannot be loaded into a playground live; `teachers.html`'s semester map is a 14-week Stats 1–2 shape, not a 7-week block.

### Rules for the phase

- **The site stays generic and public.** Nothing institution-, platform- or lecturer-specific goes on a page, and no slide text is copied. What crosses over is the textbook's conventions (naming the "textbook" dialect helps every reader of the most-used intro text), public data, and the *shape* of the worked examples, rewritten in the site's voice with the site's own numbers.
- **Both dialects, labeled.** Where textbook and software disagree (quartiles, df, z vs t) the site shows both, says which is which and why. **Never silently switch a default:** `descriptives.html` keeps type 7 (existing links, verified numbers, math-check anchors) and gains a switch.
- **Exam-first.** Every new formula appears in the form a formula sheet prints it; every new interactive has a "by hand" reading, the number a student gets with a basic calculator, beside the exact one.
- **Presets carry lectures.** Every lesson touched gains `SC.preset` params for the controls a lecture would set, documented atop its script and in `teachers.html`'s table (CLAUDE.md's rule). Two surfaces learn `?data=` so a lecturer can load lecture data from the URL without the site shipping it.
- **The iron rules carry:** frozen noise, `VIZ` math, every number verified with `node -e`, VOICE.md, `prose-lint --strict` green, `audit.js` green, `math-check.js` whenever tool math changes, the editorial checkers clean or `ACKED` with a reason.

### Crosswalk: lecture → site, today and after Phase 17

| Week (textbook ch.) | The lecture covers | On the site today | Phase 17 adds |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 (ch. 1, 3) | population/sample, cases/variables, quantitative vs categorical; mean, median, mode; quartiles, five-number summary, boxplot, 1.5 × IQR; variance and SD by hand, n − 1; density curves, N(μ, σ), 68–95–99.7, z-scores, Table A; validity vs reliability; observational vs experimental vs natural experiment | §1.1–§1.6 all exist; boxplot/IQR live in §1.4; z-explorer + 68–95–99.7 in §1.5; no Table A, no textbook quartiles, no SPSS for §1.2–§1.5 | **P79** textbook-quartile switch + five-number step-through + `?data=` + lecture dataset + SPSS entries; **P80** printed tables + lookup drill + the three table moves |
| 2 (ch. 4, 5.3) | random phenomena, sample space, the four rules, general addition rule, independence, conditional probability, general multiplication rule, Bayes + total probability; de Méré, birthday, prosecutor's fallacy, the disease-test question; mean/variance of a discrete random variable; the 8-question guessing experiment | §1.7 has LLN + disjoint rules + one conditional paragraph; Bayes only as a FAQ; `distributions.html`'s dice lab already covers the two-dice slide | **P81** §1.7 built out to the full lecture + a base-rate tree viz; §1.8 gains random variables first + the guessing experiment as a preset |
| 3 (ch. 5.1–5.3, 6.1–6.2) | binomial (n choose k, factorials, B(n, p)); sampling variability, sampling distribution of x̄, σ/√n, CLT; z-interval x̄ ± z*σ/√n, z* from Table D, margin-of-error levers, cautions | §1.8–§1.11 exist and are strong (CLT, sampling-distributions have presets); **no z-interval anywhere** | **P82** z-interval section + Interval Builder viz; formula-sheet rows |
| 4 (ch. 6.3–6.4, 7) | one-sample z-test, the five-step algorithm, one- vs two-tailed from wording, statistical vs practical significance, absence of evidence, Type I/II, β and power from two curves | §1.12 has the P-Value Explorer (takes a z); Type I/II as two bullets; power sent to Stats 3 | **P82** numbers-to-z panel, tails-from-wording, practical significance, absence of evidence, Two Curves power viz |
| 4–5 (ch. 7) | Gosset, SE, df, Table D, one-sample t CI and test, matched pairs, robustness rule, two-sample z and t, conservative df, two-sample CI, pooled vs unpooled, the overlapping-CI mistake | §1.13–§1.14 exist (t vs normal, Tug-of-War); Welch named, hand rule not; no two-sample CI; no robustness rule | **P83** SE/CI/robustness/Gosset in §1.13; three-df table + df seg + Overlapping Intervals viz in §1.14 |
| 6 (ch. 8 + 5.3, 9) | p̂, its mean/SD/SE, conditions (≥ 15 for the CI, ≥ 10 for the test, population ≥ 10× sample), CI and z-test for p, two proportions with pooled p̂; two-way tables, expected counts, components, χ², df = (r − 1)(c − 1), Table F, conditions, χ² = z² | §1.15 thorough (10-rule; χ² = z² section); §1.16 2 × 2 explorer only, no components | **P84** textbook conditions labeled; Build-a-Table r × c explorer with components, Table-F reading and `?t=` presets |
| 7 (ch. 2) | scatterplots (form/direction/strength), categorical variables in scatterplots, lurking variables, r by hand, facts about r, guess-the-r, least squares, b₁ = r·sᵧ/sₓ, b₀, r², residuals and residual plots, Anscombe, cautions | §1.17 explorer + Anscombe; §1.18 playground with residuals; no hand formulas, no residual plot, no guess-the-r | **P85** r step-through, Guess the r, hand formulas, residual-plot toggle, `?data=` on both |
| 5 and 9 (exams) | paper exams with formula sheet + tables + basic calculator; mock exams in weeks 4 and 8 | exam mode by whole course; 12 Stats 1 problems; `formulas.html` has t-based rows only | **P86** section-range exam scope + shareable mock-exam URL + 8 exam-style problems; **P87** 7-week block map, present mode, preset-table sweep |

External links the slides use that the site already replaces (a human step, see the checklist): the two-dice roller (slide 79) → `distributions.html#dice-lab`; the sampling-distribution simulator (slide 137) → `stats-1/sampling-distributions/?shape=skewed&n=10` and `stats-1/central-limit-theorem/?n=25&pop=skewed`; the probability-distribution and CLT visualizers in the notes of slides 85–92 → `stats-1/probability-basics/` and the CLT lesson.

### Running order and dates

The block started on 31 Aug 2026; the midterm is 1 Oct, the final 26 Oct. Run one prompt per session, in this order, so each lands before its week is taught or revised:

1. **P81** (probability, the week-2 lecture) — now.
2. **P80** then **P79** (week-1 material, midterm revision) — by 14 Sep.
3. **P82** (weeks 3–4: z-interval, z-test, tails, power) — by 18 Sep.
4. **P86** (section-range mock exams + problems for §1.1–1.12) — by 21 Sep, ahead of the week-4 mock exam.
5. **P83** (t procedures) — by 28 Sep. **P84** (proportions, χ²) — by 5 Oct. **P85** (correlation, regression) — by 12 Oct.
6. **P87** (instructor surface) — any time after the others; before the week-8 mock exam is ideal.

P79–P86 all touch shared registries (`faq_data.py`, `glossary-data.js`, `quiz.html`, `problems.html`, `software.js`, `teachers.html`, the search index): **one at a time.** None depends on another except P87, which documents the presets the others add.

### Notes on the slides (for Hakan, not site work)

Found while reading; none affects the plan.

- **Week 7 deck, slide 90:** the five listed points (x = 2, 3.5, 4.5, 3.5, 6; y = 6, 3, 6, 5, 8) give r = 0.68, not the 0.54 printed, and the standardized columns do not match the listed x and y (the table and the chart seem to come from different data).
- **Weeks 1–4 deck, slide 43:** the outlier rule reads "above the first quartile or below the third quartile"; it should be above Q3 or below Q1 (slides 45 and 48 have it right).
- **Slide 152:** the fraction is printed upside down (0.45/0.25); the answer 0.556 is right.
- **Slide 57:** "More on this on slide 40" points at nothing in this deck.
- **Slide 39:** "1.5+1.6/2" needs parentheses.
- **Week 5 deck, slide 22:** "15 > n > 40" should read 15 ≤ n < 40.
- **Slide 2 vs the syllabus:** room 206 vs 208.
- The tomato example's p = 4.56% comes from the table's 2 × .0228; the exact value is 4.55%. Fine for the lecture; the site's calculator will print the exact one, and P80's drill accepts the table's value.

## The human-only checklist (no AI can do these)

- [ ] **Google Search Console** — verify statscapybara.com, submit `sitemap.xml`, check weekly. Do this first; it feeds P36/P37.
- [ ] **Bing Webmaster Tools** — same, 10 minutes.
- [ ] **Backlinks** — ask colleagues to link it from course pages/syllabi (.edu links are gold); submit to **MERLOT** and **OER Commons**; answer real questions on r/AskStatistics, r/statistics, Cross Validated with links to the *specific* interactive lesson.
- [ ] **One big launch post** when the Research Toolkit track is live — r/InternetIsBeautiful, Hacker News (Show HN), relevant teaching newsletters.
- [ ] **Tell your students** — real usage + feedback beats everything above.
- [ ] **Stats 1 slides (Phase 17, Sep 2026)** — swap the decks' external links for the site's own pages (`distributions.html#dice-lab`, the sampling-distribution and CLT lessons with presets), put the preset URLs P79–P87 create on the relevant slides, and fix the slide slips listed in the Phase 17 addendum (the r = 0.54 that is 0.68, the inverted outlier rule on slide 43, the upside-down fraction on slide 152).
- [ ] Skim GA4/GSC monthly; paste interesting query data into prompt P37.
- [ ] **A real photo in the About section** — replace the "HK" initials avatar; nothing says "a human made this" like a face. Consider adding one candid line to the bio about *why* a cognitive scientist built a capybara statistics site.
- [ ] **The read-aloud test** — after Phase 10 lands, read two or three rewritten lessons aloud (or better: have a colleague or student read them cold) and mark anything that still sounds generated; feed the marks into the next P39 refresh run.

  - **2 Aug 2026 — twenty-fifth run (whose number is it: the level you analyze at, a prescription that pointed at the wrong lesson, and the glossary nothing had ever linted).** Thread: the unit you analyze at, and the fact that getting it wrong costs a false-positive rate in one direction and power in the other. `stats-2/chi-square-tests` (§2.7), `stats-4/mixed-and-multilevel-models` (§4.5), `methods/observational-designs` (§1.7), `data/wide-vs-long-data` (§2.7) and `writing/discussion-and-limitations` (§3.6), with `cheat-assumptions.html` as the hub that named the gap. **Absences, each confirmed by a sitewide grep excluding `.claude`, the generated index and AGENTS.md.** `ecological fallacy`, `ecological study`, `level of analysis`, `pseudoreplication`, `disaggregat`, `person-level`, `group-mean centering` and `Cochran` all returned **zero**; `McNemar` appeared in **exactly two files**, the poster prescribing it and one ML lesson, with the chi-square lesson the poster points at never naming it; **`hidden clustering` appeared only on `cheat-assumptions.html` and `plan.html`**, the two surfaces that tell readers to rule it out, and on no page that says what it is. **Every number was computed twice, in R 4.5.2 and independently in Node against `VIZ`.** §2.7 gained **"When the same person appears twice"**: the two meanings of independence, McNemar on 60 students (χ² = 6.00, *p* = .014; continuity-corrected 5.04, *p* = .025; exact binomial *p* = .023, all three reproduced by `mcnemar.test`/`binom.test`), discordant pairs, Cochran's Q, and the simulated cost, which is **not** the one people expect: at ρ = .6 with no real change the stacked 120-row test rejects **1.2%** of the time rather than 5% (R .0117 / Node .0132), and given a real .60 → .78 shift McNemar finds it **78%** against the stacked test's **60%**, while at ρ = 0 the two are identical at **58%** — ignoring the pairing wastes participants, it does not manufacture findings. §4.5 gained **"What ignoring the grouping costs"**: pseudoreplication named, and the design effect turned around — 20 classes of 20 at ICC .10 gives deff 2.9 and an effective *n* of 138, and a cluster-randomized null makes an ordinary *t*-test on all 400 rows fire **24.7%** of the time (16.0% at ICC .05, 37.4% at .20; R and Node agreeing to within Monte Carlo error), against 4.8% for the cluster-means test. The direction is the finding: move the treatment **inside** each cluster and the same neglected clustering turns *conservative*, 4.1% false positives and 70.9% power against a `lmer` model's 73.2%. §1.7 gained **"A fourth way to watch"** — the ecological study, the design its own list of three omitted, and the **ecological fallacy** with a worked reversal: 40 regions of 250 people with a within-region *r* = −.20 and a between-region +.80 gives an individual correlation of **−.10** and an ecological one of **+.77**, closed form .7653 and a 400-replication simulation agreeing to two decimals in both engines. §2.7 (Data) gained **"Collapsing is not pivoting"**, worked on the site's own `training-longitudinal.csv`: the naive 180-row test gives *t*(178) = 1.37, *p* = .172, the 60-athlete-means test *t*(58) = 0.81, *p* = .424 with the **identical estimate** and a standard error 1.70× larger (2.08 → 3.53, against the √2.83 the ICC of .91 predicts), while the analysis the file is actually for — the change over 12 weeks, 8.7 against 3.3 — is *t*(58) = 6.77, *p* < .001, an effect averaging the occasions together deletes. §3.6 gained the naming in its calibration list, in both directions. **FAQ freshness: three swaps, on merit.** Out: `wide-vs-long-data` #1 (**ECHO 60%, REPRISE 45%**, the worst reprise in the set — its answer nearly a copy of its own section), replaced by an unbalanced-rows question carrying the mean-of-means arithmetic (17.5 across rows vs 20 across people); out: that lesson's #3 (**TWIN question 50% / answer 22% with `tidy-data` #1**), replaced by the Excel-PivotTable question; out: `observational-designs` #3 (**ECHO 43%, REPRISE 31%**), replaced by **"How do I pick the controls for a case-control study?"**, carrying the run's fourth simulation — 100 cases, OR 2, 30% exposure: power **66% at 1:1, 79% at 2:1, 86% at 4:1, 88% at 6:1** (R and Node agreeing to a thousandth), so the fourth control is the last one worth chasing. The three new entries score ECHO 17/10/25% and REPRISE 1/0/1%. `mixed-and-multilevel-models` #1 scores **ECHO 75%** and was deliberately KEPT: its answer is REPRISE 0% and carries a litmus test the lesson does not, which is the blind spot `faq-audit.js`'s own header documents. Still **291 Q&As, 291 unique question strings, 3 per lesson**. **software.js: rotated to four untouched by runs 22–24** — `chi-square-tests`, `mixed-and-multilevel-models`, `missing-data`, `logistic-regression` — against **SPSS 32.0.0.0** (GA 23 Apr 2026, no fix pack found) and **JASP 0.98.1** (7 Jul 2026), both unchanged since run 24. Every menu label was checked against the vendor's own source rather than memory, and **one genuine defect surfaced**: the JASP logistic walkthrough said "the *Performance Diagnostics* section gives classification accuracy and AUC", but `jaspRegression`'s `RegressionLogistic.qml` shows **Performance Diagnostics holds only the confusion matrix** while Accuracy, AUC, sensitivity, specificity, precision and the Brier score sit in a separate **Performance Metrics** group; the same file shows **Odds ratios is checked by default** inside *Coefficients*, so "tick Odds ratios" was wrong too. Both fixed. Verified-correct: `jaspMixedModels` confirms *Linear Mixed Models* in a *Classical* group and the "maximal random effects structure" default pruned under Model, `jaspFrequencies` confirms *Contingency Tables* and *Multinomial Test*, JASP still has **no imputation module** (so the missing-data entry's claim stands), and SPSS 31's direct *Analyze → Descriptive Statistics → Chi-Square* dialog is real. All four APA sentences reproduce exactly: χ² = 6.6516 → 6.65 with φ = .2354 and a 90% CI of [.085, .386]; *t* = 3.3191 → 3.32, *p* = .0025; Little's χ²(48) = 54.10 → *p* = .2529; and the logistic *OR* = 2.3396 with CI [1.550, 3.531]. **Snippets: four entries extended, every R line executed here on R 4.5.2 exactly as shipped** — McNemar plus the exact binomial for §2.7 (returning the section's own 6.00/.01431/.02266), a package-free ICC/deff/effective-*n* block for §4.5 built from `VarCorr` (replacing a `performance::icc` call that cannot run on this machine), the ecological-vs-individual pair for §1.7 (seed 8, returning **−.1025 and .7655**, the lesson's published values to two decimals), and an `aggregate` demonstration for the Data lesson. **`lme4` and `lmerTest` ARE installed here**, which earlier ledger lines did not record, so the mixed-model power figure was fit rather than derived. **Link health: the outbound question, and a measurement correction.** Resolving same-course `../slug/` links properly (an earlier pass in this run mis-counted them as external) gives **40 of 97** lessons that never link forward inside their own course, which reconciles with run 24's 42. Four natural forward placements: §4.5 → meta-analysis as partial pooling over studies, §1.7 → sampling methods as the mirror problem, §3.6 → abstracts, and §2.4 → the new McNemar section. **Quips: two shortened** for the recurring trailing-clause defect (`mixed-and-multilevel-models` lost "Multilevel living", `discussion-and-limitations` lost a second sentence lifted from its own callout). **Backlog item — no Backlog section exists (P37 is still unrun), so as in runs 11–24 the item became a checker. `tools/destination-promises.js`: does a prescription's OWN named destination deliver it?** The site had checkers on both sides of this and a gap between: `audit.js` proves the link resolves, `link-promises.js` checks the **anchor text**, `prescription-terms.js` checks **sitewide** teaching. None asked whether the page a row points at contains what the row told you to run. Both of this run's poster defects sat in that gap. **The rule** takes the smallest authored unit naming both a method and a lesson and requires a lesson **that unit itself links to** to name the method — which is emphatically **not** run 22's rejected per-destination gating, because nobody guesses the destination: the author wrote it in the same row. **Two things measured:** widening the unit to the enclosing table lets a neighboring row's link satisfy this row and reports nothing at all; and a shape-2 name must not start mid-compound (`Kruskal-Wallis test` → `Wallis test` was the one false positive, 4 flags → 3). It ships with an **empty `ACKED`**, reports **64 units / 32 pairs**, and found a second defect on its first run: the poster's normality row prescribes Mann-Whitney, Kruskal-Wallis and Dunn's test while badging **§2.5, which teaches none of the three** — the row now carries both §2.5 and §2.6. **Proved on two independent defect shapes:** deleting §2.7's McNemar section fired `McNemar's test` and exit 1, removing the new §2.6 badge fired all three names and exit 1, both restores byte-identical and back to exit 0. **A sixth prose-lint surface was added in the same run: `glossary-data.js`.** 273 definitions written in the site's own voice that **no linter had ever read**, because the page scanner sees only the empty shell `glossary.html` renders into. This run typed `Analysing` into a new entry and caught it by eye, which is the surface arguing for itself; on its first run it found **`unlabelled`** sitting in the k-means definition — the same word run 16 documented its inventory as containing and still missing. Held to rule 12 only, and proved by reintroduce-and-restore. **Five glossary terms added** (pseudoreplication, design effect, ecological fallacy, ecological study, McNemar's test), taking the deck to **273**. **Verification.** `audit.js` **0 errors / 20 warnings / 64 info** (baseline), `math-check.js` **297 assertions, 0 failures**, and `prose-lint`, `faq-audit`, `widget-terms`, `link-promises`, `advice-terms`, `untaught-names`, `worked-examples`, `search-reach`, `prescription-terms`, `symbol-names` and the new `destination-promises` all **exit 0 under `--strict`**. **Zero em-dashes were added to any page**: counts held at 8 / 7 / 7 / 4 / 3 on the five subjects and 3 on the poster, while `wide-vs-long-data`'s FAQ em-dashes fell 2 → 0. **137 pages · 534 inline scripts · 235 JSON-LD blocks, 0 failures**; all shared JS, `sw.js`, the twelve tools and `faq_data.py` parse. Rebuilt index **1001 KB, 97 lessons, 37 pages**; searching it with the matcher lifted verbatim from `site.js` returned **14 HIT, 0 MISS of 14** — but only after the first pass returned **2 MISS**, `discordant` and `level of analysis`, both of them standard terms this run's own prose had paraphrased around; both are now written, which is exactly what the mandated re-search step is for. Browser pass over all changed pages via same-origin iframes at **1200px, 375px and 360px**: every expected string present, **0 console errors, 0 leading-slash links, 0 horizontal scroll at any width**, all ten link targets 200 and all four new fragments naming a real static id. Dark mode verified: bg `#0b1120`, `--link` `#6f7bf2`. The poster still fits one page, measured the P30 way: **A4 155.5 mm of 279, US Letter 155.5 of 261**, unchanged by the extra badge. **A VOICE.md read-back of the whole diff caught six things no linter sees** and all six were fixed: three "not X but Y" contrast punches rendered with semicolons or across sentences (rule 3's cousins), a "Here is where that goes wrong" drumroll (rule 4's family, uncontracted so the pattern missed it), a "this is the part that surprises people" keynote, and two mannered rule-of-three triples. Word count fell on every page it touched. **`CACHE_VERSION` bumped sc-v43 → sc-v44** (`site.js` changed and is precached). **`AGENTS.md` regenerated from `CLAUDE.md`.** **Three notes for the next run.** `glossary-data.js` was an unlinted prose surface for the site's entire life and is now covered — but the same question is worth asking of `curriculum.js`'s course subtitles and `datasets.html`'s variable descriptions, neither of which any linter reads either. The two remaining index MISSes this run (`Performance Metrics`, `effective_n`) are the **known** class the ledger already records — `software.js` and `snippets.js` are not search-index sources — and that item is now three runs old. And the forward-link number to work down is **40 of 97**, measured with same-course `../` links resolved; the naive normalizer that misses them reports 53, so any future run re-deriving it should resolve paths rather than strip prefixes.
  - **1 Aug 2026 — twenty-fourth run (the number is not the thing: measurement error, thirteen unnamed symbols, and a guide index that was hiding two-thirds of every guide).** Thread: what a single measurement is worth once you admit part of it is noise. `methods/reliability-and-validity` (§1.3), `stats-3/factor-analysis-pca` (§3.10), `stats-2/correlation` (§2.8), `methods/survey-and-questionnaire-design` (§1.9) and `guides/clean-survey-data`, the workflow where a reader actually computes the coefficient. **Absences, each confirmed by a sitewide grep excluding `.claude/worktrees`, the generated index and `AGENTS.md`.** `true score`, `classical test theory`, `standard error of measurement`, `disattenuat`/`correction for attenuation`, `communalit`, `floor effect` and `prophecy` all returned **zero**, on a site that teaches Cronbach's α across fifteen files, states the attenuation formula in full, and mentions `ceiling effect` five times. `Spearman–Brown` appeared in **exactly one file** and inside a widget subtitle at that ("That's the Spearman–Brown insight"), with the formula stated nowhere; `item-total` returned zero while `alpha if item` existed only as an SPSS column name in a `software.js` step. **Every number was computed twice, in R 4.5.2 and independently in Node against `VIZ`.** §1.3 gained **"What α buys you for one person"**: the true-score model, SEM = SD·√(1 − r), and an IQ-scaled worked case (SD 15, reliability .90 → SEM 4.74, a score of 112 sitting in [102.7, 121.3], two people needing **13 points** apart before the difference is real, and the band moving to ±6.6 at .95 and ±16 at .70), then the Spearman–Brown formula its own simulator already was: a 10-item scale at α = .70 needs k = 1.71 for .80 (18 items, landing at .8077) and k = 3.86 for .90 (39 items, .9010). §3.10 gained **"How much of an item does the model explain?"**, worked on the site's own `wellbeing-survey.csv`: `factanal` on the eight reverse-coded items gives loadings .67–.76, communalities **.44 to .58, mean .54**, χ²(20) = 20.14, *p* = .45, against a Cronbach's α of **.90** for the same items — half of each item is noise and their average is not. It also gained the parallel-analysis paragraph that the swapped-out FAQ had been carrying alone, with the number that indicts the Kaiser criterion: with 12 variables and 100 respondents of **pure noise** the largest eigenvalue averages **1.61** and **5.6 of the 12** clear 1.0 (2,000 reps; Node power iteration returned 1.613 to R's 1.610). §2.8 gained **"Undoing attenuation, and why it bites"**: the correction is unbiased enough (20,000 reps, n = 100, two 8-item scales at α ≈ .70 estimated in-sample, true *r* = .60 → observed .418, corrected .602) but **90% of its values land in [.41, .78], a spread a third wider than the raw *r*'s**, and at a true .90 it returns a value **above 1.0 in one sample in ten** (10.5% R / 10.6% Node). §1.9 gained **"When everyone picks the top box"**: a latent .50 shows as **.46** through balanced 5-point items and **.39** once two-thirds land in the top box, a latent *d* of 0.50 shows as **0.48** and **0.38**, and at 60 per group that takes power from **.74 to .54** (R `power.t.test` and `VIZ.nctCdf` agreeing to 4 dp). The guide gained what its own α = .90 buys on its own file: **SEM = 0.29** on the 1–5 composite, a 95% band of **±0.57** so 3.2 and 3.6 are indistinguishable, corrected item-total correlations of **.63 to .72** with no alpha-if-deleted above .90, the composite's real range (1.00 to 4.88, two people at the floor, none at the ceiling, no item over 19% in an end box), and the number behind "catastrophic": α **.30** before reverse-coding. **FAQ freshness: two swaps, and the second was rephrased after scoring.** Out: §1.3's "Can a measure be reliable but not valid?" (**REPRISE 25%**, its bathroom scale copied almost verbatim from the lesson's own lede), replaced by **"My scale's α is .85 but its test–retest correlation is .60. Which one is the reliability?"** (ECHO 20%, REPRISE 1%). Out: §3.10's "How many factors or components should I keep?" (**ECHO 75%**, a restatement of its own h2), replaced by **"My supervisor says 10 participants per item. Is that the rule?"**, carrying a simulation of the folklore: 12 items in 3 factors, 1,000 replications, recovered loadings off by **.086 / .064 / .037** at *N* = 60/100/300 with strong items (λ = .80) and **.184 / .118 / .067** at 60/120/300 with weak ones (λ = .50), so 100 people answering strong items match 300 answering weak ones while the 10-per-item rule passes the weak study and blocks the strong one. The first phrasing of that question TWINed **43%** with the mediation lesson's participants question and was rewritten to 13%; and because the retired FAQ was the only place on the site that named **parallel analysis**, the term moved into the lesson's prose in the same edit. Still **291 Q&As, 291 unique question strings, 3 per lesson**. **software.js: rotated to four untouched by runs 21–23** — `reliability-and-validity`, `factor-analysis-pca`, `ancova`, `one-sample-and-paired-t-tests` — against **SPSS 32.0.x** (GA 23 Apr 2026, no fix pack found) and **JASP 0.98.1** (7 Jul 2026). **Two genuine menu defects, both found by reading JASP's own module source rather than trusting memory:** `jaspReliability`'s `Description.qml` (with NEWS confirming the analysis names as of released 0.97.1) shows the classical entry is titled **Reliability** inside a *Classical* group, not "Unidimensional Reliability", and that there is **no "Inter-Rater Reliability" item at all** — rater agreement is **Rater Agreement** and the continuous case is a separate **Intraclass Correlation**. The same file shows a **Standard Error of Measurement** analysis, which is exactly the quantity §1.3 now teaches, so the walkthrough gained it as a step. Two smaller fixes: SPSS's McDonald's ω arrived in **27** (27.0.1.0, Analyze → Scale → Reliability Analysis → Model → Omega), not the "28+" the site claimed, and the factor-analysis APA sentence read "a 'assertiveness' factor". Every number in the four checked out: the α = .82, 95% CI [.78, .86] is Feldt-consistent for a 10-item scale at *n* ≈ 180; `correlation`'s *r*(58) = .42, CI [.19, .61], *p* < .001 and *r*s = .39, *p* = .002 reproduce exactly; `ancova`'s *F*(1, 57) = 6.84 gives ηp² = .107 and its adjusted means sit inside their own rounding band; `one-sample`'s *t*(24) = 2.76, *d* = 0.55, CI [1.3, 9.3] reproduce exactly and the paired *d* = 0.586. **Snippets: four entries extended, every R line executed here on R 4.5.2 exactly as shipped** — a package-free α/SEM/item-rest block for §1.3 (returning .9028, .2914, .5711 and .633–.718 on the wellbeing file), `factanal` communalities for §3.10, the attenuation correction for §2.8 (.42/√(.7·.7) = .60), and a floor/ceiling frequency check for §1.9. `psych`, `lavaan`, `multcomp` and `metafor` are not installed on this machine; `MASS`, `mvtnorm`, `car` and base `factanal` are, and everything shipped this run used those. **Link health: switched to the outbound question, as the ledger asked.** No lesson sits at 0–1 outbound prose links; the minimum is **2**, held by three lessons. The measurement worth keeping is the forward one: **42 of 97 lessons never link forward inside their own course** (course finales excluded), which is structural rather than a one-run fix. `stats-3/factor-analysis-pca` went 2 → 5 within the thread, and `methods/quasi-experiments`, the thinnest-linking lesson on the site (2 links, only one to a lesson), gained four natural placements (pre/post → within-subjects designs, matching → ANCOVA, DiD → the interaction term, natural experiments → observational designs). **Quips: four shortened** for the recurring trailing-clause defect (`survey-and-questionnaire-design`, `interactions-in-regression`, `bias-and-blinding`, `flashcards`). **Backlog item — no Backlog section exists (P37 is still unrun), so as in runs 11–23 the item became a checker. `tools/symbol-names.js`: does the site ever SAY the name of a symbol it TEACHES?** Run 23 left the note that a bare Greek letter is unsearchable, since `squash()` strips it to nothing. Asked of the whole corpus, the answer was worse than expected: **thirteen Greek letters appeared in prose and not one page using them ever wrote the name** — Spearman's **ρ** taught on ten pages with "rho" on none of them, the population mean **μ** carried from §1.1 through nine pages, plus τ, σ, θ, Σ, Φ, ω, γ, Δ, Π and ψ. Searching the built index for `Spearman rho` returned zero. **Three definitions were measured and discarded:** a per-page gate flags **54 of 126** pages and would need a 54-entry inventory; a proximity variant (name within 200 characters of a use) returns the **identical 13-flag set**, so it buys nothing; and matching the name anywhere sitewide drops the set to 7, excusing exactly the stranded reader, since γ, λ and σ are each named only on a page that does not use the letter. Twelve were fixed in prose, one naming each where it is taught, and **π is the single `ACKED` entry** (a circle constant inside √(π/2), not a taught quantity). **Proved on two independent defect shapes:** deleting the "(rho)" fired ρ and exit 1, and writing a fresh unnamed symbol (ξ) into ordinary prose fired that and exit 1, both restores byte-identical and back to exit 0. **The mandated re-search step then found a second defect, and a bigger one.** Two terms this run wrote into the guide came back MISS, and the cause was not the wording: `build-search-index.py` gave guides the **4,500-character default**, so the five long-form guides, which run 9.8k–17.5k characters, were **54% to 74% unsearchable** (`complete-worked-project` lost three-quarters of itself). That is run 13's lesson-truncation defect, still live on the pages built to answer high-intent thesis queries. **`GUIDE_MAX_CHARS = 30_000`** now covers them, and the index went 952 KB → 988 KB. **Six glossary terms added** (true score, SEM, Spearman–Brown, ceiling effect, communality, correction for attenuation), taking the deck to **268**. **Verification.** `audit.js` **0 errors / 20 warnings / 64 info** (baseline), `math-check.js` **297 assertions, 0 failures**, and `prose-lint`, `faq-audit`, `widget-terms`, `link-promises`, `advice-terms`, `untaught-names`, `worked-examples`, `search-reach`, `prescription-terms` and the new `symbol-names` all **exit 0 under `--strict`** — `untaught-names` caught one thing on the way, a "Kaiser's rule" this run had just written where the lesson elsewhere says "Kaiser criterion", which is the defect class it exists for. **Zero em-dashes were added to any page**: counts held at 4 / 4 / 4 / 9 and `clean-survey-data` fell 9 → 7, while §1.3's FAQ dashes fell 2 → 0 and the subjects grew by **383 / 316 / 474 / 273 / 229** words. **137 pages · 534 inline scripts · 235 JSON-LD blocks, 0 failures**; all shared JS, `sw.js`, the eleven tools and `faq_data.py` parse. Rebuilt index **988 KB, 97 lessons, 37 pages**; searching it with the matcher lifted verbatim from `site.js` returned **18 HIT, 0 MISS of 18**. Browser pass over all fifteen changed pages via same-origin iframes at **1200px, 375px and 360px**: every expected string present, **0 console errors, 0 leading-slash links, 0 horizontal scroll at any width**, all nine new link targets 200 and the `#wellbeing` fragment real. Dark mode verified: bg `#0b1120`, `--link` `#6f7bf2` on the new prose links. **`CACHE_VERSION` bumped sc-v42 → sc-v43** (`site.js` changed and is precached). **`AGENTS.md` regenerated from `CLAUDE.md`.** **Three notes for the next run.** The site's own search now has **three caps** (lessons 20k, guides 30k, problems/glossary 120k) and the same truncation defect has now surfaced twice, so a term the index cannot find should send you there first. The per-page half of `symbol-names` is deliberately ungated and reports **52 of 135 pages** using a symbol they never name, which is an editorial pass someone could take deliberately rather than a fault. And the outbound-link measurement above is the successor to the inbound one the ledger retired: 42 lessons that never point forward is the number to work down.
  - **29 Jul 2026 — twenty-third run (the second test, four fixes the site prescribed and never taught, and a checker for prescriptions the prose does not honor).** The ledger's standing input named `cheat-assumptions.html` as the weakest genuine teaching target, and the thread picked itself from that poster's own fix column: five pages that tell a reader what to run when the first test will not do, and stop before saying what it is. `stats-2/one-way-anova` (§2.1), `stats-2/post-hoc-tests` (§2.2), `stats-2/repeated-measures-anova` (§2.4), `stats-2/non-parametric-alternatives` (§2.6) and `cheat-assumptions.html`, the thread's hub. **Absences, each confirmed by a sitewide grep excluding `.claude/worktrees` and the generated index, with dash and apostrophe variants checked after the first grep gave a false zero on `Test–retest`.** (1) **`Welch's ANOVA` appeared in exactly two files and no lesson** — this poster's fix cell and a `snippets.js` comment — while `Brown–Forsythe` returned zero, on a course whose own practice file `study-methods.csv` is built with deliberately unequal group SDs. (2) **`Games–Howell` appeared only in `software.js`**, where an SPSS tip tells readers to click it, and **`Dunnett` returned zero sitewide**. (3) **`ε` and `epsilon` appeared in no prose anywhere**, while `problems.html` hands Problem 16 a `Greenhouse–Geisser ε = .71` as given data and `software.js`'s own APA tip says "name it and give ε". (4) **`Dunn` returned zero**, on a site that teaches Kruskal–Wallis across eleven files and never says what follows a significant one. **Every number was computed twice, in Node against `VIZ` and independently in R 4.5.2.** §2.1 gained **"When the spreads are not equal"**, worked on the site's own dataset: Levene *F*(2, 102) = 3.54, *p* = .033, ordinary *F*(2, 102) = 11.57 against Welch *F*(2, 66.43) = 13.10, both *p* < .001, the two agreeing because *n* is equal. The part the textbook warning omits was simulated at **100,000 samples per row**: at 20/20/20 with SDs 1/1/3 the ordinary *F* false-alarms **7.4%** against Welch's 5.2%; at 10/20/30 with SDs 3/2/1 it reaches **16.1%**; reverse the pairing and it falls to **2.1%**, which is the same failure pointing the other way. What Welch costs when the variances really are equal was measured rather than asserted: **about one point of power at 20 per group and three at 10** (40,000 reps). §2.2 gained **"Two cases Tukey does not cover"**: Games–Howell against Tukey on the same three groups (**.00014 vs .00004**, **.00032 vs .0017**, **.609 vs .560**), which disagree in *both* directions because the two tight groups no longer carry the noisy group's variance, and Dunnett's two-sided .05 cutoff at four groups and 20 error *df* — **2.54 against Bonferroni's 2.61 and Tukey's 2.80** — computed with `mvtnorm::qmvt` and matching the published Dunnett table exactly. §2.4 gained **"What ε is, and what it costs"**: the floor 1/(*k* − 1), and Problem 16's own *F* = 5.84 read four ways — **uncorrected (2, 22) *p* = .009, Huynh-Feldt ε = .785 → *p* = .016, Greenhouse-Geisser ε = .71 → *p* = .020, the floor ε = .50 → *p* = .034** — with the HF ε computed from the GG ε rather than quoted. §2.6 gained **"After a significant H"**: Dunn's formula, and a fifteen-value example (the numbers 1–15, so every value is its own rank) where **Dunn and pairwise Mann–Whitney disagree on the same data under the same Holm correction** — *H* = 10.5, *p* = .005; Dunn puts B against C at *z* = 2.12, *p* = .068, while three Mann–Whitney tests return *p* = .024, because every B beats every C in isolation and the two groups sit adjacent in the full ladder. **FAQ freshness: two swaps, two lessons deliberately left whole.** Out: §2.2's "Which post-hoc test should I use?" (**ECHO 100%**, every content word inside the lesson's own title, and its answer's Games–Howell advice is now a section), replaced by **"My design compares several treatments against one control. Do I still use Tukey?"** (ECHO 13%, REPRISE 4%), which carries the family arithmetic the section does not — ten Tukey pairs against four Dunnett comparisons at five groups — plus the timing constraint. Out: §2.4's "What do I do if sphericity is violated?" (**ECHO 50%, REPRISE 14%**, now answered in full by the new section), replaced by **"Mauchly's test came back non-significant. Does that settle it?"** (ECHO 0%, REPRISE 1%), which is the run's second simulation: at a true ε = .707 built from an AR(1) structure with growing variances, **Mauchly rejects 53% of the time at 12 participants, 82% at 20, 96% at 30**, while the damage stays flat at **6–7% false positives at every *n*** and Greenhouse-Geisser returns it to 5% each time. §2.1 and §2.6 kept all six of theirs. Still **291 Q&As, 291 unique question strings, 3 per lesson**. **software.js: rotated to four untouched by runs 20–22** — `repeated-measures-anova`, `non-parametric-alternatives`, `assumptions-and-when-they-break`, `independent-samples-t-test` — against **SPSS 32.0.x** (32.0.0.0 GA 23 Apr 2026, no fix pack found) and **JASP 0.98.1** (7 Jul 2026), both re-checked against the vendors' own pages rather than from memory; no menu renames since run 22. Three of the four reproduce exactly (Mauchly χ²(2) = 9.4 → *p* = .009 and *F*(1.56, 45.2) = 8.75 → *p* = .001 with ηp² = .23, which also pins the design at *n* = 30 and *k* = 3; Levene *F*(1, 58) = 8.42 → *p* = .005; the Welch *t*(57.7) = 2.30, *p* = .025, *d* = 0.59, CI [0.6, 8.8]). **One genuine defect, of the run-21 shape:** the Mann–Whitney sentence gave *U* = 245.5 with *z* = −2.13, *p* = .033 and *r* = .27, and **an exhaustive scan over every group-size pair from 5 to 60 found none that produces both** — the nearest, *n* = 18 and 42, is a lopsided design and still misses. It was rebuilt at 30 per group with *U* = 306, which reproduces **z = −2.13, *p* = .033** exactly, and now reports the **rank-biserial *r* = .32** the lesson teaches, with the *z*/√*N* value of .27 moved into the tip that already tells readers to say which one they computed. **Snippets: four entries extended, every R line executed here on R 4.5.2 exactly as shipped.** §2.1 gained `car::leveneTest` + `oneway.test(var.equal = FALSE)`; §2.2 a dependency-free **Games–Howell** built from `ptukey` with per-pair Welch df, which reproduces the section's published .609/.00032/.00014 exactly, plus a commented `multcomp::glht(..., mcp(group = "Dunnett"))` line **disclosed as taken from its documented API rather than run, since multcomp is not installed here**; §2.4 the base-R `car::Anova` route that prints Mauchly's *W*, both epsilons and both corrected *p*-values; §2.6 a base-R **Dunn's test** that returns the lesson's own −3.18/.0044, −1.06/.2888, 2.12/.0678. The three shipped R blocks were extracted from `snippets.js` and run verbatim. **Link health, measured.** The zeroes are navigation pages reached from the nav by design. `cheat-assumptions.html` was the weakest genuine target at **3** lesson/guide sources and gained five natural placements (§2.1, §2.2, §2.4, §3.8, §2.10) for **8**. `distributions.html` and `glossary.html` now sit lowest at 3, both nav-reached. **Quips: twenty-six reviewed, three shortened**, all for the recurring defect of a closing clause that only relabels the one before it (`which-test` lost "That's literally why this page exists", `cheat-assumptions` lost "never after", `datasets` lost "it has nowhere to be"). **Backlog item — no Backlog section exists (P37 is still unrun), so as in runs 11–22 the item became a checker. `tools/prescription-terms.js`: does the site tell a reader to RUN a method it never TEACHES?** `advice-terms.js` asks this of `software.js` and `checks.js` and stops there; this extends it to the four prescription surfaces it excluded — the posters' fix column, `problems.html`'s worked solutions, and `which-test.html`/`plan.html`'s leaves. The rule gates the **whole name-plus-head-noun pair** rather than the surname, which is load-bearing: "Welch" is taught, so a surname gate misses the defect the tool was written for. **Three things measured, not guessed:** dash normalization is load-bearing (without it four of the best-taught names on the site flag on nothing but en-dash-versus-hyphen); a bare capitalized token gave **22** flags and **10** even after a stem gate, all ordinary English and scenario names, against **1** for the compound-or-head-noun rule; and `snippets.js` comments were added, measured (617 → 1,006 chunks, one extra flag, `Iglewicz-Hoaglin`, which `data/outliers-in-practice` teaches) and dropped. It ships with an **empty `ACKED`** and found one thing on its own: `problems.html` labels a step **Welch–Satterthwaite degrees of freedom** and the site joined those two names nowhere else, so §2.2 now names the formula. **Proved on two independent defect shapes:** deleting §2.1's Welch section fired `Welch's ANOVA` and exit 1, and writing a fresh unexplained prescription into a poster fix cell (`Hartley's test`) fired that and exit 1, both restores byte-identical and back to exit 0. **Verification.** `audit.js` **0 errors / 20 warnings / 64 info** (baseline), `math-check.js` **297 assertions, 0 failures**, and `prose-lint`, `faq-audit`, `widget-terms`, `link-promises`, `advice-terms`, `untaught-names`, `worked-examples`, `search-reach` and the new `prescription-terms` all **exit 0 under `--strict`**. **Zero em-dashes were added to any page** — per-page counts held at 8 / 7 / 8 / 4 and `cheat-assumptions.html` fell 4 → 3, with §2.4's FAQ em-dashes falling 1 → 0 — while the four lesson subjects grew by **436 / 300 / 290 / 363 words**. **137 pages · 534 inline scripts · 235 JSON-LD blocks, 0 failures**; all shared JS, `sw.js`, the ten tools and `faq_data.py` parse. Rebuilt index **942 KB, 97 lessons, 37 pages**; searching it with the matcher lifted verbatim from `site.js` returned **19 HIT, 0 MISS of 19**, including from the poster. Browser pass over all seven changed pages via same-origin iframes at **1200px, 375px and 360px**: every expected string present, **0 console errors, 0 leading-slash links, 0 horizontal scroll at any width**, all ten link targets 200 and all five new fragments naming a real static id. Dark mode verified: bg `#0b1120`, `--link` `#6f7bf2`. The poster still fits one page, measured the P30 way: **A4 155.5 mm of 279, US Letter 152.1 of 261**. **`CACHE_VERSION` bumped sc-v41 → sc-v42** (`site.js` changed and is precached). **Two notes for the next run.** The index-search harness has a trap worth knowing: `flexRe` must be fed `norm(query)`, not the raw string, because its own `[^a-z0-9 ]+` strip removes **capital letters**, and the `pages` half of the index is an array of `{u, txt}` objects while `lessons` is a plain slug→text map — getting either wrong produces confident false MISSes, which it did twice here before the harness was fixed. And **a bare Greek symbol is not searchable at all**: `squash()` strips it to nothing, so `ε` returns zero however the corpus is written, which is why the new section spells "epsilon" out. Worth a decision next lap: teach symbols by their spelled name, or widen the matcher.
  - **29 Jul 2026 — twenty-second run (the picture and what it licenses you to conclude, four folk rules turned into numbers, and a checker for terms the site's own search cannot find).** The ledger asked for a thread, and this one picked itself: five pages that hand the reader a visual judgment and never say where the line is. `stats-1/visualizing-data` (§1.4), `stats-2/assumptions-and-when-they-break` (§2.5), `writing/tables-and-figures` (§3.3), `stats-4/meta-analysis` (§4.10) and `which-chart.html`, the thread's hub and the weakest inbound target on the site. **Absences, each confirmed by a sitewide grep excluding `.claude/worktrees` and the generated index.** (1) **`prediction interval` returned zero sitewide**, on a lesson whose own prose says "the diamond's width *is* its confidence interval" and stops there. §4.10 gained **"The diamond is not a forecast"**: for a literature shaped like the playground's defaults (*k* = 8, pooled *d* = 0.40 with SE 0.10, τ̂ = 0.20, I² ≈ 50%) the diamond runs **[0.20, 0.60]** while the 95% prediction interval runs **[−0.15, 0.95]**, so a new trial finding nothing is entirely consistent with the literature — and the interval is wider than the true-effect distribution it describes ([0.01, 0.79]) because with 6 df the multiplier is 2.45, not 1.96. (2) **`Sturges` and `Freedman–Diaconis` appeared only inside §1.4's own FAQ answer, named and never stated**, and `Scott's rule` was absent entirely, on the lesson whose whole interactive is a bin dial. §1.4 gained **"How wide should a bin be?"** with the three formulas worked on the widget's own bell (400 observations, SD 13, range ≈ 80 → **10, 13 and 17 bins**, bracketing the dial's default of 12), plus the counterintuitive part that had to be measured: on the two-peak shape Scott and Freedman–Diaconis both fall to **9** bins, *fewer* than they asked for on the bell, because the gap between the humps inflates the SD and the IQR alike. A claim that the coarser bins therefore hide the peaks was **simulated and abandoned** — at 9 bins the dip is plainly visible (counts 86/82/30/81/86), and it only disappears once the peaks are closer than about 2σ, where the true density is unimodal anyway. (3) **`Cumming`, `arm length` and `standard error bars` all returned zero**, while §3.3 asserted twice, in prose and again in its own FAQ, that "overlapping bars don't automatically mean no difference" without a number. §3.3 gained **"What overlapping bars actually mean"**: for two independent groups of 30, ±1 SE bars that just touch give *t* = 1.41, ***p* = .16**, 95% CIs that just touch give *t* = 2.83, ***p* = .006**, and *p* = .05 sits where two 95% CIs **overlap by 59% of one arm** or two SE bars **clear each other by 0.83 SE** — proportions that barely move with *n* (.007 at 20/group, .006 in the limit). The section ends on the case that breaks the rule outright: 20 people scoring 50 and 54 with a between-person SD of 12 give 95% CIs of **[44.4, 55.6] and [48.4, 59.6]**, near-total overlap, against a paired ***t*(19) = 4.47, *p* = .0003**. (4) **`quantile-quantile`, `envelope` in the Q-Q sense and `pointwise` returned zero**, on a lesson that told readers "if the points fall on a straight line, the data is normal" and "trust your eyes". §2.5 gained **"How crooked does normal data look?"**, and the sentence it walks back is now "behaving like a normal sample". At *n* = 20 the top point is **drawn at 1.87** and genuinely normal data lands it anywhere in **[1.29, 2.71]** while the middle point holds [−0.38, 0.26], so **the ends wobble 2.2× the middle**; and **49% of perfectly normal samples at *n* = 20 (66% at *n* = 50) put at least one point outside a pointwise 95% band**. **Every number was computed twice, in Node against `VIZ` and independently in R 4.5.2**, agreeing throughout (Q-Q envelope 1.29/2.71 vs 1.32/2.78 at 40k reps and 1.29/2.71 at 200k; spurious-bimodality 62.5% R vs 62.0% Node, 11.2% vs 11.3%; the overlap arithmetic and the paired *t* to 4 dp; bin rules against R's own `nclass.Sturges`/`nclass.scott`/`nclass.FD`). **`which-chart.html` gained the branch it was missing**: the chooser had no route for a within-subjects design and sent those readers to a grouped bar, which is exactly the display §3.3 now warns about. A **Slopegraph (paired lines)** leaf, its thumbnail, and a cheat-table row; the `bar` leaf's mistake string now carries the .16/.006 pair. **FAQ freshness: two swaps and one revision, scored with `faq-audit.js` then judged by hand.** Out: §3.3's "Should error bars show SD, SE, or a confidence interval?" (**ECHO 60%** against its own h2 "Say what the error bars show", **REPRISE 22%**, and its closing caution is now a whole section), replaced by **"My figure is in color. Does that matter?"** — `colorblind`, `grayscale`, `black and white` and `line style` all returned zero, and the answer carries a number computed from the site's own palette: the red and green it uses for good-versus-bad status, #ef4444 and #22c55e, have a grayscale contrast ratio of **1.65:1** against the 3:1 accessibility asks. Out: §1.4's "How many bins should a histogram have?", which the new section now answers in full, replaced by **"I only have 15 data points. Is a histogram still the right picture?"** (Sturges asks for 5 bins at *n* = 15, and **62% of 50,000 normal samples show a spurious second peak** at that setting against 11% at *n* = 400). And §2.5's normality FAQ was **revised rather than swapped** — it ended on "trust your eyes", which the new section contradicts — so it now points at the calibration instead. Still **291 Q&As, 291 unique question strings, 3 per lesson**. **software.js: rotated to four untouched by runs 19–21** — `meta-analysis`, `factorial-anova-two-way`, `post-hoc-tests`, `transformations-and-recoding` — against **SPSS 32.0.x** (GA 23 Apr 2026, no fix pack) and **JASP 0.98.1** (7 Jul 2026), both re-checked against the vendors' own pages rather than from memory; JASP's `rowMeanNaRm` was confirmed against its release notes. **Two genuine defects, both of the run-21 shape.** The 2×2 walkthrough reported simple effects of *F* = 15.2 and 0.31 beside a main effect of 9.31 and an interaction of 6.87, and **in a balanced 2×2 those four are locked together**: *F*_A + *F*_A×B must equal the two simple effects, 16.18 against 15.51. Holding the published effect sizes fixed, the simple effects are **16.09 (*p* < .001) and 0.09 (*p* = .76)**. And the meta-analysis sentence gave *k* = 18 studies of *N* = 3,842 with τ = 0.21 and a CI implying SE = 0.071, which **no set of 18 studies that large can produce** (that SE implies about 85 people per study, N ≈ 1,530). It was rebuilt from an actual DerSimonian–Laird fit over 18 studies totalling **N = 2,104**: *d* = 0.42, 95% CI [0.29, 0.55], *z* = 6.28, *Q*(17) = 38.62, *p* = .002, I² = 56%, τ = 0.21 — and it now carries the **95% prediction interval [−0.04, 0.89]** the lesson teaches. `post-hoc-tests` was checked and needed nothing (its two Tukey pairs reproduce *q* = 5.05 → *p* = .003 and *q* = 5.67 → *p* < .001 exactly). The Box–Cox tip's "usually half a unit wide or more" was replaced by the measured widths from `MASS::boxcox` (**0.8 at *n* = 50, 0.6 at *n* = 100**). **Snippets: three entries extended, every R line executed here on R 4.5.2 exactly as shipped** (`nclass.*` returning 10/13/18 on the shipped generator; `car::qqPlot(residuals(fit), envelope = .95)` on car 3.1.5). The exception is disclosed: **`metafor` is not installed here**, so `predict(res)` was taken from its documented API rather than run. A scan of all 77 snippets for deprecated APIs found nothing beyond run 20's single known false positive. **Link health, measured.** No teaching page sits at 0 inbound; the zeroes are navigation pages reached from the nav by design. `which-chart.html` was the weakest genuine target at **2 lesson sources** and gained five natural placements (§3.3, §1.3, §2.5-data, §2.4, §2.8) for **7**. **Quips: nine reviewed, two shortened**, both for the recurring defect of a closing clause that only relabels the one before it (`tables-and-figures` lost "Same three points, taller lie.", `descriptives` lost "The capybara will not judge it."). **Backlog item — no Backlog section exists (P37 is still unrun), so as in runs 11–21 the item became a checker. `tools/search-reach.js`: can the site's own SEARCH find the things the site TEACHES?** Run 13 found by hand that 87 of 97 lessons were truncated in the index and a quarter of the site's prose was unsearchable; the prompt has asked every run since to re-search the index for the terms it just wrote. This generalizes that step to all **262 glossary headwords** — the site's own declaration of what it teaches — and runs in about a second, **lifting `norm`/`squash`/`flexChar`/`flexRe` verbatim out of `site.js` by brace-matching** so it tests the shipped matcher rather than a copy. **Two stricter definitions were measured and discarded**: splitting compound headwords gave an identical 0 misses, and checking each term against the specific lesson its own back-link names flagged **46 of 262**, every spot-check a phrase artifact (`Alpha` against a lesson that writes α, `1.5 × IQR rule` against one that writes `1.5 × IQR`, `k-means clustering` against one that writes `k-means` nineteen times) — an 18% rate needing a 46-entry `ACKED`, which is the inventory VOICE.md rule 12 calls the mistake. **It found two genuine defects on its first run, both fixed in `site.js`.** **`Cramér's V` returned ZERO hits, accent typed or not** — `norm()` strips diacritics from the query while the corpus is matched raw, the mirror image of run 21's apostrophe bug — so `ACCENT_FOLD` now lets each squashed query letter accept its own accented forms (0 → 7 hits, all 24 control queries identical). And **`Wilks lambda` returned nothing**, because the site writes `Wilks' Λ (lambda)` and no flat one-separator budget crosses that gap; `flexRe` now allows up to three separators **at the reader's own word boundaries** while keeping one inside a word, which is the safe version of the change run 21 measured and rejected — `[^a-zA-Z0-9]{0,3}` cannot swallow a letter, so a match still never crosses a whole word. Over 31 queries only `Wilks lambda` 0 → 2, **`90% CI` 0 → 3 (run 21's other standing item)**, `p value` 46 → 58 and `test data` 2 → 4 moved by more than one; `ab`/`xy`/`zz`/`qq`/`test`/`data`/`mean`/`sd`/`Cohen's d`/`chi-square`/`of the`/`in a` were identical and 20 full-corpus passes stayed at 11 ms. Verified in the live overlay: Cramér's V 7 results, Wilks lambda 2, 90% CI 3, with the glossary card, the typo path (`hetroscedasticity` → Heteroscedasticity) and the honest dead end all unchanged. The checker ships with an **empty `ACKED`** and was **proved on two independent defect shapes** — blanking `ACCENT_FOLD` fired `Cramér's V` and exit 1, reintroducing the pre-run-13 glossary cap fired **26** terms and exit 1, both restores byte-identical and back to exit 0. Its own first run also caught **`Scott's rule` as a once-only eponym** in `untaught-names.js`, fixed by naming the bin rules in `descriptives.html` beside the rule that page actually uses. **Verification.** `audit.js` **0 errors / 20 warnings / 64 info** (baseline), `math-check.js` **297 assertions, 0 failures**, and `prose-lint`, `faq-audit`, `widget-terms`, `link-promises`, `advice-terms`, `untaught-names`, `worked-examples` and the new `search-reach` all **exit 0 under `--strict`**. **Zero em-dashes were added to any page** — per-page counts held at 7 / 9 / 6 / 6 and `tables-and-figures` fell 7 → 6 — while the four lesson subjects grew by 309 / 298 / 353 / 275 words. **137 pages · 534 inline scripts · 235 JSON-LD blocks, 0 failures**; all shared JS, `sw.js`, the eight tools and `faq_data.py` parse.
