  - **23 Jul 2026 — ninth run (the last never-refreshed inferential core, and a linter for the spelling problem).** The staleness ranking, run as "last commit that substantively changed this file, mass passes excluded", cleared to `stats-1/independent-samples-t-test` (26 Jun), `stats-2/regression-diagnostics` (27 Jun), `stats-3/manova` (3 Jul) and, from a genuine same-commit tie in the 4 Jul tier, `stats-1/effect-size-and-power` (P3) and `methods/from-question-to-hypothesis` (P4). **Three absences, each confirmed by a sitewide grep rather than assumed.** (1) **Independence.** `regression-diagnostics` listed "the four things to check" and omitted independence entirely, while `assumptions-and-when-they-break`'s own FAQ answers "which assumption matters most?" with **"Independence, without question"** — two Stats 2 lessons contradicting each other. Worse, `Durbin-Watson` appeared **only in `software.js`**, so the SPSS walkthrough told readers to tick a statistic no lesson explained. The list is now five, with a section on the check the residual plot cannot make: autocorrelation (with what the statistic's 0-to-4 range means, verified against the statsmodels docs) and clustering, handing off to `mixed-and-multilevel-models` as the repair. (2) **Hedges' g: zero hits sitewide**, on a site with an effect-size converter, an effect-size lesson and a meta-analysis lesson, and with §1.13's formula printing "/ standard deviation" without saying *which*. §1.13 now names the pooled SD and the small-sample correction. (3) **MANOVA had no effect size at all** — not "η²", not the phrase — on a lesson that lists four test statistics and a follow-up strategy. §3.11 gained η² = 1 − Λ^(1/s) with the two-group collapse worked through. Also: §2.10 stopped conflating leverage with influence and now hands both to `assumptions-of-regression`, which owns the FAQs for each; §1.12 finally names the Mann-Whitney fallback its own normality bullet implied; §1.13 points at the post-hoc-power warning that lives in §3.9; §1.1 reaches `variables-and-operationalization` and `preregistration-and-open-science`, the mechanism its own "before you collect data" refrain needs. **FAQ freshness:** five swaps, each displacing an answer that restated its lesson's prose, and **four candidates were killed by the duplicate check before being written** — a Cook's-distance answer (§3.8 owns it), a Likert t-test-vs-Mann-Whitney answer (§2.6 owns it), an optional-stopping answer (QRP owns it) and an exploratory-analysis answer (preregistration owns it). What shipped: "My two groups are men and women, not randomly assigned. Does the t-test still work?" (the arithmetic is identical and that is the trap), "How do I know whether a pattern in my residual plot is real or just noise?", "Does a significant MANOVA protect my follow-up ANOVAs from multiple testing?" (the protected-F myth; **"protected F" returned zero hits sitewide**), "I found a significant effect with a small sample. Doesn't that make it more impressive?", and "My hypothesis has three parts. Is that one hypothesis or three?" (which bridges Methods to the family-definition machinery run 8 wrote into §2.2). **Every number was derived twice.** The winner's-curse answer rests on 300,000 simulated studies per cell: with a true d = 0.30 and 20 per group, power is **15.3%** and the significant studies average **|d̂| = 0.82**, nearly three times the truth — and the same grid recomputed through `VIZ.nctCdf` gives **15.2%**, matching the simulation to 0.1 pp at every n from 10 to 50. The Hedges figures come from J = 1 − 3/(4df − 1) (**4.2%** at n = 10/group, **2.0%** at 20, **0.8%** at 50), and the simulation's mean d̂ reproduced d/J to four decimals independently. The MANOVA arithmetic (Λ = 0.62, two groups → η² = .38) and the lineup-protocol attribution (Buja et al., 2009) were both checked before publishing; a **Breusch-Pagan-in-GLM claim was dropped for being unverifiable rather than wrong**, following run 3's precedent. **software.js — run 8's five outstanding entries all verified, and one real gap closed.** SPSS 32 docs (Apr 2026) confirm `Analyze → GLM → Univariate` unmoved; JASP's `Assumption Checks → Sphericity tests` current; `psychometric-functions`' PSE = −b₀/b₁ and JND = ln(3)/b₁ re-derived from the logistic. The gap: **SPSS's Linear Regression dialog has no robust standard-error option**, confirmed by IBM's own support page and a 2026 AMPPS tutorial written because of it — while `regression-diagnostics` prose recommends robust standard errors as the fix for heteroscedasticity, sending students hunting for a checkbox that isn't there. Two tips added (the SPSS routes that do work; and that Durbin-Watson tests order, not clustering). **Snippets — one snippet that could not run.** `regression-diagnostics`' Python imported matplotlib and statsmodels, created an unused figure, and then called `fit.fittedvalues` with **`fit` never defined**. It now fits the model the way its own R half does, and both halves gained a Durbin-Watson line so the new prose has something runnable behind it. **Cross-pollination:** practice-problem back-links **46 → 51 of 57**, adding `assumptions-of-regression`, `multicollinearity-and-variable-selection`, `roc-curves-and-auc` (ML now 2), `generalized-linear-models` and `nonsignificant-results`. Every description was checked against the problem's actual sub-questions first, which is what stopped §3.8's line from simply restating §2.10's (both cite Problem 22, so §3.8's foregrounds the case-31 leverage/Cook's-D judgment instead). **Six remain:** outliers-in-practice, reproducible-workflows, preregistration-and-open-science, types-of-data, psychometric-functions, and `guides/clean-survey-data` (already linked by the guides' own convention). **Quips:** ten reviewed, eight kept, two rewritten — `regression-diagnostics` said "mirror" twice in two sentences with the second explaining the first, and `manova` was a summary ending in the "That's ‹topic›." formula that three quips in this batch shared. **Backlog item — stop finding British spellings one run at a time.** Runs 2, 6 and 7 each found another survivor of the 22 Jul word-list sweep, and run 7 declared the site "verifiably at zero"; this run found **`flavour` in four more files**, including the Stats 4 course landing page. Rather than fix four more words, **VOICE.md gained HARD RULE 12 and `prose-lint.js` a `britspell` pattern** (budget 0, driven by an explicit `BRIT_SPELLINGS` inventory — an inventory, not an `-ise`/`-our`/`-re` rule, because run 2 proved those cannot be generalized without turning *analyses* into "analyzes"). On its first run it immediately caught **three more** that all four hand sweeps had missed: `modelling` in `assumptions-and-when-they-break`, a `<h2>Flavours of each</h2>` heading in `reliability-and-validity`, and `unravelling` in `informed-consent-and-irb`. Sitewide count is now **0 and checked**, including FAQ answers linted from `faq_data.py`, which is the exact path the original regression travelled. `analyses`, `enrolled`, `enrolling`, `programmed` and `analogue` are deliberately excluded and documented as such in three places. **Verification:** `audit.js` **0 errors / 21 warn / 63 info** (baseline unchanged), `math-check.js` **270 assertions, 0 failures**, `prose-lint --strict` **exit 0** with **zero em-dashes added to any of the five** (9→9, 7→7, 9→9, 8→8, 3→3) and §2.10's FAQ trio improving 3 → 2. All shared JS, `faq_data.py`, `sw.js` and both tools parse; **22 changed pages · 88 inline scripts · 43 JSON-LD blocks, 0 failures**; all 228 internal links and fragments on the changed pages resolve. One probe artifact, diagnosed rather than believed: my link checker reported `power.html?sc=anova` broken, which was the query string, not the file.
  - **23 Jul 2026 — eighth run (the never-refreshed core lessons).** The staleness ranking, run as "last commit that substantively changed this file, voice/meta/OG mass passes excluded", cleared to five lessons no previous run had touched as its subject: `stats-2/post-hoc-tests` and `assumptions-and-when-they-break` (27 Jun), `stats-3/logistic-regression` and `mediation-and-indirect-effects` (27 Jun), and `stats-4/bayesian-thinking` (29 Jun). **Three topics turned out to be missing from the whole site, not just from these pages** — a sitewide grep, not a guess, returned zero hits for each. (1) **False discovery rate.** `Benjamini`, `false discovery` and `FDR` appeared nowhere across every HTML, JS and Python file, on a site whose §2.2 "why it matters" callout already named genomics and brain imaging, the two fields that abandoned family-wise control precisely because of this. §2.2 gained a section on it with a worked ten-p-value comparison (Bonferroni asks all ten to clear .005 and returns one; BH asks .005/.010/.015/… and returns three at a stated ~5% false share), plus a callout on which rate to control and why the choice must be declared. (2) **Baron and Kenny.** Zero hits, on the recipe every mediation textbook still teaches. §3.6 gained a section retiring step one, with the two reasons a real indirect effect can sit under a null total effect (power for the total is not power for the paths; an opposite-signed direct path cancels part of the sum). (3) **MCMC.** Zero hits across two Bayesian lessons, while §4.2's own prose credited "modern computing" without saying what the computing does. It now explains why the lesson's Beta example has a closed form and realistic models do not. Also: §2.5's "big three" omitted **linearity** entirely and never linked its own sibling `regression-diagnostics`, both fixed; §3.7 had no inferential content at all despite its SPSS box telling readers to read the omnibus χ² and Nagelkerke R², so it gained the section that explains them plus the Wald-vs-likelihood-ratio caveat; §3.6 now reaches `causal-dags-and-confounding` with the assumption the three-regression recipe never states (randomizing X does not randomize M). Tool links added where they earn their place: `power.html?sc=anova`, `descriptives.html`, `effect-sizes.html` (OR ↔ *d*), `distributions.html` (the Beta the §4.2 playground draws). **FAQ freshness:** five swaps, each displacing an answer that restated its own lesson's prose, and **two candidates were killed by the duplicate check before they were written** — a "Welch by default" answer for §2.5 (§1.10 already owns it, in two of its three answers) and a residuals-vs-raw-data answer (§3.8 already owns it). What shipped instead: "Do I have to correct for every test in my whole paper?" (defining the family is a judgment you state, and redrawing it after seeing the p-values is p-hacking with arithmetic), "Can I let the assumption test decide which test I run?", "How many participants does a mediation analysis need?", "Why is my odds ratio in the millions, with a confidence interval to match?" (separation), and "How strong is my prior, in units of data?". FAQ em-dashes across the five fell 11 → 7. **Every number in them was simulated or derived, not recalled.** The Levene-then-choose answer rests on 200,000 samples per cell: with n = 45 and 15 and the larger variance in the smaller group, always pooling gives **15.2%** false positives at a nominal 5%, Welch gives **4.9%**, and letting Levene choose gives **5.7%** — better than pooling, still not what it advertises; with equal variances Welch costs **0.05 to 0.12 percentage points** of power, which is the honest reason the default is free. Mediation power came from a percentile-bootstrap simulation (500 replications × 1,000 resamples per point): two medium paths (a = b = .39) reach 80% at about **70**, two small paths (a = b = .14) at about **550**, against **398** for detecting a single *r* = .14 — so the product costs more than either half. That simulation independently reproduces Fritz & MacKinnon's published 78 and 558, and the Fisher-*z* helper reproduces the site's own G\*Power anchor (*r* = .3 → 84.9 ≈ 84). Separation was demonstrated by hand-rolling Newton-Raphson on eight perfectly split points: the odds ratio passes **4 million** by iteration 10 and the Hessian goes singular by iteration 20. The Beta answer is exact rather than simulated — a Beta(α, β) prior is worth α + β trials, which is literally what §4.2's "prior confidence" slider sets — and was **read back off the live page**: at the defaults the posterior mean is .654 = 17/26, and at confidence 80 it is .540. **software.js — the backlog item was a missing entry, not drift.** `assumptions-and-when-they-break` carried an R/Python snippet but **no SPSS/JASP walkthrough**, sitting in the audit's info list as "fine if conceptual" when it is the single most software-dependent topic in Stats 2 ("where is the Q-Q plot in SPSS?"). It now has one (Explore → Normality plots with tests, Spread vs Level with Levene, ZRESID/ZPRED for the regression case; JASP's Assumption Checks in both t-test and ANOVA, and the residual Q-Q that replaces one plot per group), taking the site to **34 entries**. Four of run 7's outstanding nine were verified with no changes needed (`logistic-regression`, `effect-size-and-power`, `multicollinearity-and-variable-selection`, `generalized-linear-models` — SPSS 27+ Power Analysis, Collinearity diagnostics, and the GLM Type-of-Model tab are all long-stable, as are the JASP Power and GLM modules). **Tally 28 of 34 verified; the five outstanding are** factorial-anova-two-way, repeated-measures-anova, simple-linear-regression, power-analysis-for-complex-designs, psychometric-functions. Tips were added where the new prose created an obligation: the FDR note on §2.2, and separation plus Wald-vs-LR on §3.7. **Snippets — one genuine error.** §2.5's Python labelled a line "Welch fallback for unequal variances" and then called `scipy.stats.f_oneway`, which is Fisher's equal-variance ANOVA; its own trailing comment admitted as much. It now calls `pingouin.welch_anova`. §2.2 gained `p.adjust(method = "BH")` and `multipletests(..., method="fdr_bh")` so the new FDR section has something runnable behind it. The other three lessons' snippets checked clean. **Cross-pollination:** back-links **41 → 46 of 56**, adding `assumptions-and-when-they-break` (Problem 17, the two clinics whose SDs are 3.1 and 6.9) plus five orphans — effect-size-and-power, independent-samples-t-test, regression-diagnostics, `ml/classification-metrics` (taking ML off zero) and `writing/writing-results`. Every pb-xref description was checked against the problem's actual sub-questions, and the first draft for §1.13 was rewritten after that check showed it described the wrong three sub-questions. **Ten remain**, now concentrated in data (1/3) and the two stats-3/stats-4 pairs. **Quips:** ten reviewed, eight kept, two rewritten — `effect-size-and-power`'s "isn't just significant. The effect size is massive" was VOICE.md's banned contrast punch with a period doing the dash's job (quips are linter-exempt, which is exactly why they need reading), and `writing-results`' was a 30-word summary rather than a line, the same defect run 2 fixed on two other pages. **Verification:** `audit.js` **0 errors / 21 warn / 63 info** (info fell 64 → 63: one fewer missing SOFTWARE entry), `math-check.js` **270 assertions, 0 failures**, `prose-lint --strict` **exit 0** with **zero em-dashes added on any of the five** and every page inside budget (§4.2 started at exactly 10 of 10 and stayed there). All shared JS, `faq_data.py` and `sw.js` parse; **10 changed pages · 40 inline scripts · 20 JSON-LD blocks, 0 failures**; all 77 internal links and every `#fragment` on the changed pages resolve. Browser pass on desktop light, desktop dark and 375px mobile: **zero console errors across all ten pages, zero horizontal scroll at 375px**, every new h2 picked up by the mini-TOC, both new quips live, and the injected blocks confirmed showing the new SPSS entry, the FDR tip, the separation tip and the fixed Python snippet. **`CACHE_VERSION` bumped sc-v26 → sc-v27** (two QUIPS changed and `site.js` is precached). **One process note:** the first in-browser probe reported the new tips and snippets missing, which was the probe's fault, not the site's — `software.js`'s `tips` render in the **APA block**, not the `.software` block, and the snippet block defaults to its R tab so Python text is absent from `innerText` until the toggle is clicked. Re-probed against the right containers, all four landed. Run 4's lesson generalizes: check what the negative result actually measured before believing it.
  - **23 Jul 2026 — seventh run (the research-integrity block).** `plagiarism-authorship-and-citation`, `privacy-and-confidentiality`, `ai-in-research-ethics`, `fraud-and-self-correction` and `methods/the-replication-crisis`. **This run found the three defects a freshness loop exists to catch, none of which any gate can see.** (1) **An authoring instruction had shipped:** the replication-crisis lesson's low-power bullet ended "(link this to <a …>effect size &amp; power</a>)" — a note-to-self that survived into production, on an already-linked phrase. It is the only one on the site; a sitewide sweep for `TODO`, `FIXME` and similar found nothing else. (2) **A tool link pointed at the wrong tool:** the fraud lesson described statcheck as "roughly the idea already built into our tables calculator", but `tables.html` contains **zero** statcheck or consistency logic while `apa.html` has the actual recompute-and-warn check. Repointed, with a sentence saying what it does. (3) **The site's most time-sensitive claim had dated.** `ai-in-research-ethics` is deliberately built to age well (it names no tool, no version, no dated policy), but it rested on "a language model predicts plausible text; it does not know facts, look things up, or calculate" — which stopped being the whole story once assistants routinely search and run code. The pedagogy survives and the rule is unchanged, so the fix keeps the point and adds the nuance that matters: you usually cannot tell from the answer which mode produced it, a retrieved source can still be summarized wrongly, and code still runs on whatever inputs you gave it. The dependent bullet, the interactive's feedback text for the "ask AI for a statistic" case, and the FAQ on invented references were all updated to match, since leaving any of them would have made the lesson contradict itself. **FAQ freshness:** four swaps plus that consistency fix. The Ethics FAQs were the least repetitive of any tier so far, so the swaps went to genuinely absent questions: "How much do I have to change a sentence for it to count as a paraphrase?" (there is no word count, and the test is procedural), "Can I share my data publicly if I promised confidentiality?" (the open-data collision, decided at consent time or not at all), "I think I have found an error in a published paper. What should I do?" (assume a mistake, write it down precisely, author first then editor then PubPeer), "Does a failed replication mean the original was wrong?" (four things that produce a non-replication, only one of which is a false positive), and — replacing the one FAQ that named specific products — **"Is it unfair to use AI to improve my English if I am not a native speaker?"**, which the site had never addressed despite it being a live anxiety for much of its audience. **Drift:** four more of run 6's outstanding thirteen verified with no changes (post-hoc tests, non-parametric alternatives, regression diagnostics, survival analysis; SPSS's Post Hoc/Games-Howell, Nonparametric Tests + Legacy Dialogs, ZRESID/ZPRED + Cook's + Durbin-Watson, and Kaplan-Meier/Cox are all long-stable, and the JASP paths including the Survival module are current). **Tally now 24 of 33; the nine outstanding are** effect-size-and-power, factorial-anova-two-way, repeated-measures-anova, simple-linear-regression, multicollinearity-and-variable-selection, logistic-regression, power-analysis-for-complex-designs, generalized-linear-models, psychometric-functions. **Cross-pollination:** back-links **40 → 41**; remaining 16 listed in this run's output, still weighted to data (1/3), ml (0/2) and writing (2/4). **Quips:** all five reviewed. Ethics quips are deliberately earnest (CLAUDE.md records the decision that serious lessons should not carry a flippant line, which is why they print hidden), so the register was left alone; two that read as 22-word summaries rather than lines were tightened, and the three with a real turn — privacy, fraud's "distrusts water that's suspiciously clear", and the replication crisis's "Then remembered the first three" — were left exactly as they were. **Backlog item — finishing the spelling normalization for good.** Run 6 fixed `artefact` and `enrol`; this run found the list was still incomplete and swept the rest: **`honour`** (4 files, including the homepage accessibility statement and a JS string in an interactive), **`cancelling`/`cancelled`** (8), **`unravelled`**, plus `standardised`/`centred`/`colour` left in two generator scripts' comments. **21 more replacements across 13 files, and the site is now verifiably at zero** — a scan for the full British inventory returns nothing outside `snippets.js`, whose `color="grey"` and `"grey40"` are ggplot and matplotlib literals that must not change, the same exclusion the original sweep recorded. `enrolled`, `enrollment`, `programmed` and `analogue` were checked and correctly left, being valid American forms. **Verification:** `audit.js` **0 errors / 21 warn / 64 info**, `math-check.js` **270 assertions, 0 failures**, `prose-lint --strict` **exit 0**; all shared JS, both Python generators, and **27 changed pages'** inline scripts and JSON-LD parse; every prose link and `#fragment` on the five lessons resolves; **the AI lesson's eight-case interactive was driven end to end in the browser** to confirm the rewritten feedback string renders and the case still scores correctly; zero console errors. **`CACHE_VERSION` bumped sc-v25 → sc-v26** (two QUIPS changed, and `site.js` is precached). It now reaches forward to the CLT (the law says where the proportion settles, the CLT describes the shape of the wobble on the way), to sampling distributions and confidence intervals from its own closing callout, and it gained **a section on conditional probability** — the lesson taught four rules and omitted P(A | B), which is the one the whole Bayesian strand and every association test rest on. §1.9 gained the two calculators that postdate it (`descriptives.html` builds the interval from pasted data, `tables.html` supplies the critical value); §4.1 gained the confidence-intervals link its own percentile-interval section implied, a cross-validation link, **and a paragraph on BCa intervals** (percentile intervals are biased for skewed or biased statistics, which the lesson never mentioned); §4.3 and §4.5 gained the `pb-xref`s they never had, plus the Beta distribution in `distributions.html` and the design lesson that decides whether you need a mixed model at all. **FAQ freshness:** five swaps, each displacing an answer that restated the lesson's prose. The new ones: "Can I tell whether two groups differ by seeing if their confidence intervals overlap?" (**derived, not recalled** — non-overlap does imply p < .05, but the converse fails, and for two equally precise means the difference reaches p = .05 while the intervals still overlap by **29.3%** of their length; 200,000 random configurations produced **zero** counter-examples to the non-overlap direction), "A test is 99% accurate and I tested positive. Am I 99% likely to have the disease?" (**recomputed exactly**: 1-in-1,000 prevalence, 99% sensitivity, 1% false-positive rate → 99 true and 999 false positives out of 1,098, so **P(ill | positive) = 9.02%**), "My bootstrap interval and my t-interval disagree. Which one do I trust?", "How do I test a hypothesis with a credible interval?" (the ROPE), and "Why doesn't my mixed model print p-values?" (the lme4 denominator-df question, with Satterthwaite/Kenward-Roger and the likelihood-ratio alternative). Only the *track* got blockified (it's a flex item of `.cw-bar-row`); the fill stayed `display:inline`, and inline boxes ignore `width`/`height`, so every bar measured **0×0** — a grey rail with a count and no fill. Fix: **`display:block` on `.cw-bar-fill`** (one declaration). Verified the fill now measures **194×16 px**, and the whole pipeline drives end-to-end: raw 106 rows / 12 distinct labels / mean 115.5 (999s included) → all four steps on → **100 rows (raw 106), 2 labels (Control 59 / Treatment 41), mean 60.2, 9 missing** (4 planted + 5 range-checked), log lists all four ops. **(2) `writing/abstracts-and-titles` "Abstract grader" rewrite** — the *scoring* half was never broken (moves, warnings, word count and grade all rendered); the dead part was the before/after reveal. `#ag .ag-ba-body { display:grid }` is an **author rule, so it outranks the UA stylesheet's `[hidden] { display:none }` regardless of specificity** (cascade *origin* beats specificity) — the panel was permanently visible (measured 1195 px tall *with* the `hidden` attribute set), so the button toggled an attribute that did nothing. Fix: **`#ag .ag-ba-body[hidden] { display:none }`**. Verified closed on load (display:none, 0 px) → click reveals the red "Vague — invisible" / green "Findable — informative" cards → click again re-hides, button text round-trips. **SWEEP (all 50 toolkit lessons, nothing else dead).** Both bugs are greppable *classes*, so I scanned all 50 statically for each — a **validated** detector (it flags exactly these two on the pre-fix tree via `git stash`, zero on the fixed tree), finding **no other instance of either**. Also `node --check`ed all 50 inline viz scripts (**0 syntax errors**) and ran a **runtime probe** over all 50 (same-origin iframes at `?embed=1`, checking `window.onerror`, blank-canvas detection by pixel-sampling every `.viz canvas`, unpopulated `—` readouts, and zero-boxed inline elements): **no JS errors, no blank canvases, no collapsed elements.** One probe hit was a **false positive worth recording: `methods/sampling-methods`** shows "Avg of draws / Bias / Spread (SD)" as `—` on load — these are *accumulators over repeated draws* and are empty **by design** until you draw; confirmed correct (after "Draw ×40": avg 47.9% (40), bias +0.1 pts, SD 4.9 pts — a near-zero bias being the right answer for simple random sampling). No precached shell asset changed → no `CACHE_VERSION` bump.

  - **17 Jul 2026 — shipped as Stats 4 §4.11 (appended, no eyebrow renumbering); audit 0 errors/21 warnings, prose-lint clean (6 em-dashes/1k ≤ budget, no pattern hits), verified in-browser (light + dark + mobile + embed), zero console errors.** New lesson `stats-4/psychometric-functions/` frames any 2-choice task (proportion of "long"/"yes" vs stimulus level) with **temporal bisection** as the worked example: fit logistic / cumulative-Gaussian / Weibull by MLE, read off **PSE** (50% point) and **JND** (half the 25–75% span), and dial a manipulation that either **shifts the PSE** or **changes the slope**, shown as a two-curve baseline-vs-manipulated compare. **Interactive** (`pf-canvas`, frozen-noise: one uniform draw per condition×level×trial-slot, sliders re-analyze the same trials, "New trials" reseeds): a 2-param Nelder–Mead maximizes the binomial likelihood live; readouts for both conditions' PSE/JND/slope + ΔPSE; family selector + compare toggle + trials/σ/shift/×noise sliders. **Math independently verified via `node -e`:** all three curve equations are textbook-exact (F(PSE)=.5000, F at ±JND = .2500/.7500, slopes exact); PSE/JND recovery from a known Gaussian observer (μ=500, σ=80) is exact for the matched family (PSE 500.0, JND 54.0 = σ·0.6745); the "logistic fit to Gaussian data reports a JND ~10% smaller" claim confirmed (48.4 vs 54.0 = 10.4%). **One prose correction the verification caught + fixed:** the family-choice callout (and matching FAQ) originally said fitting *all three* families moves the PSE "by a millisecond or two" — true for the symmetric logistic/Gaussian (~1 ms), but the **asymmetric Weibull sits a robust ~8 ms off** (the genuine global MLE, stable across 400 restarts and the whole μ range, and visible in the interactive itself: Weibull PSE 528 vs 520 for the other two). Reworded both to distinguish the symmetric pair from the Weibull (whose offset is *because* it's a zero-anchored detection function fit awkwardly to symmetric bisection data) — a more accurate and pedagogically richer point, and now prose and interactive agree. **Round-trips all pass** (slider away-and-back, family logistic→weibull→gauss→logistic, compare on/off — every readout byte-returns). **Standard integration** done: curriculum.js (ready, appended → Stats 4 now 11), 3 checks, 3 FAQs (+inject-faqs), a QUIPS line, **3 glossary terms** (PSE, JND, psychometric function), a `c:"stats-4"` quiz question, R+Python snippet (logistic-GLM PSE/JND + psignifit/quickpsy pointer), a SPSS/JASP+APA `software.js` entry (binary logistic → PSE=−b₀/b₁, JND=ln3/b₁; condition×duration interaction = PSE-shift vs slope-change), sitemap, homepage ItemList (Stats 4 → 11) + static og/twitter counts (95→96) + CLAUDE.md count. - [x] P66 · guard scripts — tools/math-check.js gate + tools/extlinks.js for P38 (Powerful · Extra)
  - **21 Jul 2026 — both shipped; the site's math passed clean on the first run.** `tools/math-check.js`: **218 assertions, 0 failures, 0.84 s** (zero-dependency; `viz.js` loaded in a `vm` under a `window`/`document` shim). Every assertion cites its published source and takes that source's rounding precision as its tolerance. Coverage: normal family + symmetry identities; `tInv`/`chiSqInv`/`fInv` vs printed table criticals (t.975,10 = 2.2281, χ².95,3 = 7.8147, F.95,3,20 = 3.0984, …) plus cross-distribution identities (χ²α,1 = z².975, F.95,1,20 = t².975,20, the F reciprocal rule); the statcheck case t = 2.05, df = 28 → .0498 plus **252 quantile↔CDF round-trips** over a 7×6 α/df grid; `gammaln`/`gammp`/`betai` identities exercising *both* `gammp` branches and *both* `betacf` branches; density integrals ≈ 1 and pdf-vs-CDF derivative checks; all four **G*Power anchors reproduced exactly** (64/group · 84 · 159 · 88) plus ncp = 0 collapse-to-central; and the documented tool-page constants. Two method notes worth keeping: the χ²(1) and F(1,·) integrals need the **x = u² substitution** (plain Simpson can't handle their x^−½ singularity — this was a bug in the *test*, caught during the build), and `erf(0)` lands ~1e-9 off zero because A&S 7.1.26 is a rational approximation, so its tolerance is the formula's own 1.5e-7 bound. **Negative-tested four ways** (perturbed erf coefficient → 3 failures, gammaln Lanczos coefficient at 1.3e-5 relative → 27, `tUpper`'s 0.5 factor → 11, `invUpper` 200→12 bisection iterations → 143, dropping `nctCdf`'s `normCdf(−δ)` term → 3); every section is load-bearing, and `viz.js` was restored byte-identical after each. `tools/extlinks.js`: the site's whole external surface is **10 unique URLs** — **7 ok** (GA tag, JASP, RUG profile, schema.org, both Scholar variants, the ko-fi button image), **2 bot-blocked-known-fine** (ko-fi 403, LinkedIn 999), **0 broken**. The `ALLOW` table is status-specific so a genuine 404 can't hide behind a familiar failure (negative-tested: flipping LinkedIn's allowed status to 200 reclassified its real 999 as BROKEN with an explanatory note); planted dead links (bad host + a 404 service) were both flagged with correct file attribution and `--strict` exited 1. One false positive fixed during the build: `xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg"` in `offline.html` is a namespace identifier, not a link (w3.org 403s this script), now excluded by context rather than by a hardcoded namespace list. No site files changed — no `CACHE_VERSION` bump needed. **Noted, not actioned:** a stale git worktree `.claude/worktrees/cool-shaw-e6dba5` is present again (P38's July run removed a different one).

Tell me" beside the About link in every footer — a `mailto:` whose subject carries the page's own path, read from `<link rel="canonical">` so a report names the clean public URL (`/stats-2/correlation/`) even when the page was served from a subpath. Verified on **119 of 120 pages**; the one without it is `404.html`, which is self-contained by design. (2) **PWA shortcuts:** three in `site.webmanifest` — Continue learning → `progress.html`, Statistics Toolbox → `toolbox.html`, Course quiz → `quiz.html` — with relative `url`s, verified under the `subpath` server to resolve against the manifest's own URL and stay inside `scope`. (3) **Section share-links:** a copy-link button on every `h2` of every lesson and guide — **473 buttons across 101 pages**, swept at **360px** with **0 layout shift, 0 horizontal overflow, 0 errors**. `ensureH2Ids()` was extracted from `renderTOC()` so the TOC and the share-links share one id scheme; its dedupe now checks every id on the page, and a pre-flight scan of all 111 lesson/guide files found **0 slug collisions**, so no existing anchor moved. Two things the verification actually caught and changed: the sweep found **3 headings** (psychometric-functions, sampling-methods ×2) where an in-flow button wrapped to a line of its own in a narrow column, fixed by switching below 700px to an absolutely positioned button in a permanently reserved right gutter — still zero shift, and the re-sweep came back clean; and focus reveal was moved from `:focus-visible` to **`:focus`**, because a keyboard user could otherwise land on an invisible control. Confirmation is a green-check icon swap in a fixed box rather than a "Copied!" label (which would widen the button and shove the heading), so `flashCopied()` gained a `data-copy-flash` visually-hidden live region for the wording — the existing text copy buttons were regression-checked and behave byte-identically. Hidden in `@media print` (verified by the P30 CSSOM flip). (4) **View transitions — DROPPED, and the reason is not "it broke".** `@media not (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce) { @view-transition { navigation: auto } }` was written and parsed correctly (confirmed in the CSSOM, and the browser supports the feature), but **no transition ever ran**: the preview pane serves its document with `visibilityState === "hidden"`, and Chrome skips view transitions for hidden documents. A top-level opt-in behaved the same, which isolates it to the environment rather than the media nesting. So the two risks the prompt named — the SW's `controllerchange` auto-reload firing mid-transition, and a dark-theme flash — could not be observed at all, and shipping an unverifiable visual change to every page of the site is a bad trade for a cross-fade. The removed rule and the reasoning are left as a comment in `styles.css` where it would go. `CACHE_VERSION` **sc-v17 → sc-v18** (site.js, styles.css and site.webmanifest are all precached). Gates: `audit.js` PASS (0 errors), `math-check.js` PASS (218/218), `prose-lint --strict` exit 0.

  - **21 Jul 2026 — all five items shipped; the GA decision was made empirically, not by argument.** (1) **`privacy.html`** — a ~900-word plain-language page (0 em-dashes, 0 prose-lint hits): what is collected (one anonymous page view), the full `localStorage` inventory as a table (`sc-progress` / `sc-last` / `sc-checks` / `sc-exam` / `sc-cards` / `sc-name` / `theme`) with an honest note that device-local means a phone shows none of a laptop's progress and that clearing site data wipes it, the two external services a page contacts (Google Analytics, the Ko-fi button image) and what each can see, the never-list (no ads, accounts, selling, dark patterns), a children/classrooms note for instructors, and the footer feedback mailto as the contact route. **The "What is collected" section is deliberately written to absorb P70's event list in one sentence.** (2) **GA posture — Consent Mode SHIPPED, not just disclosed.** `gtag('consent', 'default', …)` with all four storage types `denied` was added before `gtag('js')` in all **134** GA-carrying pages (byte-identical block; `offline.html` has no GA by design). **Verified in-browser rather than assumed:** with cookies cleared, a full load writes **no `_ga` cookie at all**, and the `page_view` still reaches `/g/collect` with `gcs=G100`. The cost, stated plainly here and on the page: each pageload gets a fresh `cid` with nothing persisted, so **per-page view counts (what P37/P39 actually read) stay accurate while Users/Sessions become unreliable** — a good trade, and it also means no cookie banner is owed. (3) **Claims sweep** — `teachers.html`'s twitter:description ("no tracking") and `toolbox.html`'s progress bullet reworded to what is true; `progress.html`'s "no account and no server" and `teachers.html`'s "nothing is sent to a server" scoped to progress, each now linking the privacy page, and `teachers.html` gained an explicit paragraph naming the analytics so instructors read it before assigning. Final sweep returns **only** the planning docs describing the problem plus one unrelated "untracked choices" in the p-hacking lesson. (4) **Footer:** `renderFooterPrivacy()` / `.footer-privacy`, BASE-aware (verified resolving `../../privacy.html` → `/privacy.html` from lesson depth). (5) **README rewritten** — the old "51 lessons across 5 courses" is gone, and it now points at `curriculum.js` and the homepage for live counts rather than baking new numbers that will stale the same way. Registered as a root page everywhere *except* `TOOLBOX` (it is not a tool): audit `ROOT_PAGES` + a new **`NON_TOOL_ROOT`** set so its two-level Home → Privacy breadcrumb is named correctly in the error text, prose-lint `ROOT_PAGES`, `SEARCH_PAGES` (searching "privacy" finds it — confirmed live), `QUIPS`, `build-search-index.py` (36 pages), `sitemap.xml`. `CACHE_VERSION` **sc-v19 → sc-v20**. The set: **`search_used`** (overlay opened, `page_type`, never the query), **`viz_png_export`**, **`print_used`**, **`feedback_click`** (all `page_type`), **`lesson_embed_view`** (`with_preset`), **`pwa_installed`**, **`quiz_practice_finished`** + **`exam_finished`** (`scope`, `length`, `score_bucket`), **`problem_solution_opened`** (`course`), **`cert_downloaded`** (`course`). **Verification was network-level, not gtag-spy-level:** each event was read back out of the real `google-analytics.com/g/collect` request (`en=` plus its `ep.`/`epn.` params) via `performance.getEntriesByType("resource")`, because the preview's own network log is same-origin-only and GA batches multi-event hits into a POST body. Confirmed on the wire: `search_used`/`viz_png_export`/`print_used`/`pwa_installed` from a lesson, `feedback_click` (`ep.page_type=lesson`), `lesson_embed_view` flipping `with_preset` **false → true** between `?embed=1` and `?embed=1&n=40`, `quiz_practice_finished` (`scope=stats-1`, `length=10`), and `exam_finished` whose **`scope` string is byte-identical to the `sc-exam` key** (`stats-1+stats-2`) with the retry run correctly reporting `scope=retry`, `length=9`, and writing nothing to `sc-exam`. **Three suppressions were tested, not assumed:** `problems.html`'s "Print with solutions" force-opening all 43 solutions fired **zero** events (the `mode` guard) and reverted to the reader's own 3; reopening one solution fired once, not twice (the `seen` map); and a double `beforeprint` counted once while a genuine second print run counted again (`trackPrint`'s `active` flag, which exists because Safari drives print off the media query and others off `beforeprint`). `pageType()` resolves all five kinds — verified home/tool/lesson/guide/course by same-origin iframe sweep. **Adblock case: `window.gtag` deleted entirely, then every instrumented surface exercised** — lesson search/PNG/print/feedback/install, a full practice quiz (4/10 rendered), a full exam (1/10, `sc-exam` still written), problems open + both print modes, cert download (filename still produced). **Zero console errors, zero broken features.** `privacy.html` extended as P69 designed it to be: "one thing" → "two things", a five-bullet plain-language event list with the three **Never**s bolded (never the query, never the actual score, never the name on the certificate), plus a note that no identifier is attached beyond a page view's non-persisting one. - [x] P71 · search v2 — typo tolerance, glossary instant answers, zero-result states (Powerful · Extra)
  - **21 Jul 2026 — shipped, and the ranking fixes came from running the case list rather than from reasoning about it.** Three helpers do the forgiving matching: `norm()` (case/diacritics/hyphens), `squash()` (separator-free, so `chi-square` === `chisquare`), and `flexRe()` — a regex over the squashed query allowing one optional separator between characters. `flexRe` **replaced** the old `toLowerCase().indexOf()` full-text scan and is **faster than what it replaced** (~1–2 ms vs ~2–3 ms over the whole 620 KB corpus, because the old scan re-allocated every lesson's text per keystroke), so the responsiveness requirement was met by *removing* work and **no debounce was added**; measured end-to-end per-keystroke cost across the case list was **0.9–7 ms**. Typo tolerance is `within1()`, Damerau-Levenshtein ≤ 1 (transposition included — "teh" is the real typo), on title/term tokens only; full text stays exact-modulo-separators by design. **A first pass over the written case list found three genuine defects that were then fixed:** (1) "bonferoni" returned nothing at all, because the glossary headword rule only stripped a trailing parenthesis and nobody types "bonferroni correction" — added a one-word-query-vs-first-token match; (2) "corelation" put three cheat sheets above the Correlation lesson, because keyword hits were hard-capped at 50 and so a *fuzzy* keyword outranked a fuzzy title — replaced the cap with `aside()` scaling (×0.55), which keeps an exact keyword above a guessed title spelling while dropping a fuzzy keyword below it; (3) "what is power" returned zero, since "what" and "is" match no title — the title pass is now re-run on `askTerm()`'s stripped term when a question form comes back empty. After the fixes: "corelation" → Correlation & Regression Calculator + the Correlation lesson, "regresion" → Simple Linear Regression + Regression Diagnostics, "what is power" → 40 results led by the three power pages. **Glossary instant answers** lazy-load `glossary-data.js` beside the index (confirmed loading BASE-aware from lesson depth) and render a definition card above the results; its `glossary.html#<term>` deep link was verified to **land on the right `<details>` and open it** (`#power-statistical` → "Power (statistical)"). **Zero results give three states, all verified:** "Did you mean:" + up to 3 near titles (`boostrapp` → Bootstrap & Resampling; `corelasion` → the two Correlation pages), a glossary-only hit ("That term is defined above…" for `hetroscedasticity`, whose def card still carries the lesson link), and a true miss (`asdfghjkl`, `zzzqqq`) — each ending in quiet Browse-the-glossary/toolbox links. **Case list run in full: 5 typos, 3 spacing/hyphen variants, 3 glossary questions, 2 nonsense strings**, plus 6 distance-2 typos to exercise the did-you-mean branch that the original 13 never reached (their misses were genuinely far from any title). **Keyboard verified, not assumed:** the def card's links are in the Tab trap in DOM order (the trap now queries the whole panel, not just the list), arrows still drive the results, arrows drive the *suggestions* on a did-you-mean screen while the two browse links stay outside the selection (`wireRows()` only wires anchors below `searchMatches.length`), and Escape closes and restores focus to the search pill. `?q=` deep link verified from lesson depth with relative hrefs intact. Light + dark + **360 px with no horizontal scroll**, zero console errors. `CACHE_VERSION` **sc-v21 → sc-v22**. Gates: `audit.js` PASS (0 errors, 21 warnings = baseline), `math-check.js` 218/218, `prose-lint --strict` exit 0.
- [x] P72 · capstone guide — one dataset, the whole research journey (Extra Powerful · Ultra)
  - **21 Jul 2026 — shipped, and the independent verification agent earned its keep by catching three defects in the author's own audit trail.** `guides/complete-worked-project/` ("One Study, Start to Finish", ~3,050 words) carries **`memory-2x2.csv`** end to end across eight steps: research question → operationalization → 2×2 between-subjects design + power → data checks → assumptions → two-way ANOVA → simple effects with effect sizes and CIs → APA paragraph → limitations + the ethics review. **`sleep-experiment.csv` was deliberately avoided** (it stars in the JASP guide); the 2×2 was picked over the correlation and logistic files because it supports the richest honest arc, and no other page on the site quotes its numbers except `datasets.html`, whose worked solution the guide matches exactly. **Two imperfect moments, both load-bearing rather than decorative.** (1) *The underpowered wish:* the design needed **N = 128 (32/cell)** for a medium interaction and the file has **25/cell**, giving **70% power**, stated in the design section and again in the limitations. The guide also names the trap that produces most bad 2×2 proposals — plugging k = 4 into an ANOVA power calculator returns **N = 179**, which powers the *omnibus* test, not the interaction — and gives the fix as a memorable rule: a 2×2 interaction with effect *f* is powered like a **two-sample t with d = 2f** at the same total N, so `power.html` → Two-sample t → d = 0.50 → 64/group → **N = 128**. That instruction was verified **live on power.html itself**, which prints "you need 64 per group (128 total)", and the equivalence was checked numerically (agrees to <0.001 power units for N ≥ 40; the claim is scoped to that range in the prose because it breaks down below N ≈ 40). (2) *The outlier that isn't:* the 1.5×IQR rule flags **four points in one cell only** (recall 3, 15, 16, 17), purely because that cell's IQR is 2.0; their within-cell z-scores are −1.91 to 2.32 and the cell's SD matches its three neighbours. Deleting them would push the interaction from **F(1,96) = 8.27, p = .005, ηp² = .08** to **F(1,92) = 12.62, p < .001, ηp² = .12**, i.e. *toward* the hypothesis, which is exactly why they stay. **Verification was genuinely independent:** a separate agent recomputed every statistic from the CSV without reading the page's own verification comment, using two independent routes for the ANOVA. Every reader-facing number verified. It found **three errors confined to the HTML verification comment** — the encoding p-value written as `1.4e-21` when the true value is `1.7948e-20`, the deep simple-effect p written as `1.1e-6` when it is `8.4772e-7` (both had been transcribed as plausible approximations rather than computed values), and a residual excess kurtosis of `−0.1511` computed with a **hybrid estimator** that mixes an /n fourth moment with an /(n−1) variance (the G2 that pairs with the G1 skew on the same line is `−0.0356`). All three were fixed after reproducing them independently. The agent also surfaced a **latent library bug**: `VIZ.fUpper` underflows to exactly 0 for d1 = 1 and F ≳ 110 (`betai` cancellation), so the encoding p-value is unobtainable by the method the comment claimed; the comment now records the `2*tUpper(√F, df)` identity as the correct route, and the library fix is flagged as separate work since no rendered number depends on it. Two prose claims were tightened on the same evidence: the power-equivalence range, and an instruction to paste partial η² into `effect-sizes.html`, whose η² is a *total* η² (.03 here, not .08). Cross-page agreement confirmed against `datasets.html`, `descriptives.html`'s actual formulas (type-7 quantiles, G1 skew, t-based CI), `tables.html`, and `apa.html` — whose own statcheck `recomp` returns OK on all five rendered F/t triples. **Registrations: seven, not the six CLAUDE.md documented** — `tools/prose-lint.js` keeps its own `GUIDES` list mirroring the audit's, and a guide missing from it goes silently unlinted; CLAUDE.md now says so. Cross-links added to `plan.html`'s plan card (a fifth "See a full worked project" button, verified rendering and resolving), a new `teachers.html` section framing it as a semester-project template, all four existing guides' Keep going footers, and the Methods / Stats 2 / Writing landing pages. Browser-verified: zero console errors, nav dropdown entry BASE-aware from lesson depth, `?q=` search returns it as the top hit, 9 section share-links, both mock tables scrolling inside themselves with **0 page-level horizontal overflow at 360px** (measured against an existing guide as a control), light + dark. `CACHE_VERSION` **sc-v22 → sc-v23**. Gates: `audit.js` PASS (0 errors, 21 warnings = baseline), `math-check.js` 218/218, `prose-lint --strict` exit 0 (7 em-dashes against a budget of 10, no pattern hits).
- [x] P73 · de-AI v2 — second baseline, VOICE.md v2 + prose-lint v2 (Powerful · Extra)
  - **15 Aug 2026 — the law and the measuring stick only; no content changed** (`git diff --stat` touches `VOICE.md`, `tools/prose-lint.js`, `CLAUDE.md`, `PROMPTS.md`, `ROADMAP.md` and nothing else). **`prose-lint --strict` is now EXPECTED RED — 168 violations — and stays red until P77 closes the paydown. `node tools/audit.js` remains the commit gate** (PASS, 0 errors, 20 warnings = baseline).
  - **What tightened.** Rule 1 gained a second clause: **≤ 4 em-dashes per page AND ≤ 8 per 1,000 words of that page**, both gating separately so the failure says which one missed. The rate clause is the one that bites where the flat cap is toothless — `descriptives.html` carries 10 dashes in 291 words, where ≤ 4 would still be triple a human editor's rate; its effective cap is 2. Rule 2 fell **≤ 3 → ≤ 1** across a lesson's FAQ trio. **New rule 13** gives the five injected surfaces their first dash budgets, gated from now on: `checks.js` ≤ 50, `software.js` ≤ 15, `glossary-data.js` ≤ 30, inline-`<script>` literals ≤ 130, `snippets.js` comments ≤ 5. **QUIPS are exempt permanently** — the anti-rule makes them brand voice, so the table prints `exempt` and the gate skips them.
  - **The report now prints the lesson MEDIAN and an at-the-cap count beside every budget**, because "every page passes" is exactly the reading that hid this: 17 pages sit at exactly their cap today, 36 lessons at exactly their FAQ cap. **P74–P76 aim at a lesson median of ≤ 2, not every page at 4.**
  - **Three report-only metrics, gated deliberately never** (faq-audit's precedent — gating a judgment metric teaches the next run to write around it). **Overcorrection watch:** per-page semicolons (549, lesson median 3) and ellipses (33, median 0), so a paydown can be checked for removing the tell rather than relocating it. **Bold-lead bullets:** 447 across lessons+guides on 81 of 102 pages (`--bold`). Three narrower definitions were measured and dropped — a colon inside the `<strong>` gives 117, a colon after it 12, either side 133 — so the colon is a punctuation habit, not the shape; every li/strong spelling returns the same 447. **The addendum's hand count of 403 came from an ad-hoc scan whose definition wasn't recorded; the linter's is in the script, so future comparisons mean something.** **Cross-page duplicates:** 122 shingles → **49 merged passages** (`--duplicates`). Three properties were measured, not assumed: stripping the baked `<footer>` first (207 shingles → 125; without it every long page collides on its own footer tail, and it is stripped *only* for this scan, since removing it from `extractProse` would shift every page's word count and break comparison with this baseline), requiring ≥ 6 real words in a window (otherwise the report fills with APA statistics that legitimately repeat — shared *numbers*, not shared prose), and merging overlapping windows (one repeated sentence otherwise reads as five problems). Most of the 49 are honest (a cheat poster mirrors `which-test.html`'s tree by design; a page naming another page's title matches its title); the genuine repeats for P74–P77 are `power.html` + `stats-3/power-analysis-for-complex-designs` ("halving the effect you're chasing roughly quadruples the sample you need"), `stats-2/one-way-anova` + `stats-2/post-hoc-tests`, `stats-2/factorial-anova-two-way` + `stats-3/interactions-in-regression`, and `ethics/questionable-research-practices` + `methods/the-replication-crisis`.
  - **Stated in VOICE.md so it survives future sessions: build no generic "AI word" inventory.** Measured on this corpus — delve 0, "worth noting" 0, "keep in mind" 0, "at its core" 0, crucial 7, journey 4 — and the two words a stock list flags hardest are subject vocabulary here: **leverage 54×** (high-leverage points), **robust 42×** (robust standard errors). Rule 12's polarity lesson, generalized: measure a candidate in context first, and prefer a shape rule to a word list.

  - **Why.** Stats 1–4 were organized by difficulty. Nine courses became **eight**; 97 lessons became **102**.

  - **24 lessons changed course** — chi-square/correlation/simple-linear-regression 2→1, effect-size-and-power 1→3, the eight regression and ANCOVA lessons 3→2, all twelve Stats 4 lessons 4→3 — and **42 of the 47 existing lessons were renumbered** (only stats-1 §1.1–1.5 kept their numbers).
  - **25 redirect stubs**, one per moved URL plus `stats-4/` itself. GitHub Pages cannot serve a 301 and there is no `_redirects` equivalent, so a stub is a noindexed, canonicalized page with an instant meta refresh **and** a JS `location.replace` that forwards `location.search` and `location.hash` — which is what keeps old `?embed=1` iframes and `SC.preset` deep links working, since a meta refresh alone drops the query string. No stub targets another stub; all 25 land in one hop, verified under both `serve.js` roots.
  - **Five new lessons**, each fully integrated: `stats-1/producing-data-and-sampling-design` (§1.6), `stats-1/binomial-distribution` (§1.8), `stats-1/inference-for-proportions` (§1.15), `stats-2/inference-for-regression` (§2.7), `stats-2/nonlinear-relationships-and-transformations` (§2.17).

Poster and `which-test.html` badges were renumbered *from their own `href`s*, which is mechanical rather than a judgment call.

Worth rebuilding as a real tool if lessons are ever reordered again.

  - **`hotdog-contest-2019.csv`** is the ninth practice dataset and the first that is not simulated, so the page's "every file is generated" note was corrected rather than left standing. **Problem 16** works a five-number summary and a boxplot sketch by hand, and shows R's quartiles reaching the same verdict from different numbers. Also fixed in passing: `problems.html`'s meta descriptions still said "Forty-three" after P64 and P81 had taken it to 46.
  - **`software.js` gained the first four Stats 1 weeks** (types-of-data, describing-data, visualizing-data, z-scores), which the first SPSS seminars cover and which had no entry at all. Writing them surfaced a shape worth knowing: **`tips` render inside the APA block**, so an entry with no `apa` shows no tips, which is why `types-of-data` carries its interval-versus-ratio point in its steps.
  - **§1.6 now links out to `methods/quasi-experiments`** (natural experiments, the textbook's chapter 3 case that sits between observation and a real experiment) and says what `methods/reliability-and-validity` actually separates. Glossary gained *Quartile* and *Five-number summary*.
  - Gates: `audit.js` 0 errors, `math-check.js` 623/623, every editorial checker clean or ACKED, no page-level horizontal scroll at 360px on the three touched pages.
  - **Found and NOT fixed, because it is outside this prompt: `AGENTS.md` has drifted badly from `CLAUDE.md`, which it claims to mirror.** It still says the site has *297* math-check assertions and *43* practice problems, still describes `descriptives.html` as type-7 only, and is missing the whole P73 prose-lint v2 and P74 readout-placeholder sections; P80 and P81 did not update it either. Patching P79's paragraphs into it would make it accurate about one prompt and wrong about eight. The fix is a deliberate regeneration from `CLAUDE.md` (the only intended differences are the header lines and the `AGENTS.md`/`Codex` name swaps), which is worth its own small session.
- [x] P80 · Stats 1 wk 1–2 — printed Tables A/D/F + look-it-up drill in tables.html, the three table moves in §1.5 (Powerful · Extra)
- [x] P81 · Stats 1 wk 2 — the probability lecture: general rules, conditional, Bayes + base-rate tree; random variables before the binomial (Extra Powerful · Max)
- [x] P82 · Stats 1 wk 3–4 — σ-known z-interval + z-test, tails from wording, practical significance, absence of evidence, Two Curves power viz, formula-sheet rows (Extra Powerful · Max)
  - **The missing rung is in.** §1.11 gained "When σ is known: the z-interval" (x̄ ± z*·σ/√n, the three z\* values off the foot of P80's Table D, the margin of error and its three levers, n = (z*σ/m)² rounded up, and the SRS/known-σ cautions), and §1.12 gained "Where the statistic comes from" (z = (x̄ − μ₀)/(σ/√n)) plus the textbook's five-step algorithm. Both run on one worked case, a filling machine at 227 g with σ = 5 and four boxes averaging 222: the interval is [217.1, 226.9] and the test is z = −2.00, two-sided p = .0455, and the lessons say out loud that the interval excludes 227 for exactly the reason the test rejects.
  - **Three new interactives.** **Interval Builder** (§1.11) assembles the interval in the original units and prints the by-hand line beside it. **From numbers to z** (§1.12) is a calculator panel that owns no picture: it drives the P-Value Explorer *through the explorer's own slider*, dispatching the input event a hand would, so there is no second copy of the p-value math. **Two Curves** (§1.12) draws the null and the truth on one axis with α, β and power shaded; its verified anchor is μ₀ = 22.0, σ = 0.5, n = 5, α = .05 two-sided, μ₁ = 22.6 → cutoffs 21.562/22.438 and **power = .765** (and power collapses to exactly α when μ₁ = μ₀, asserted in the browser run). Both halves of power are computed as **upper tails through `VIZ.normQ`** rather than by subtracting from 1, per CLAUDE.md's far-tail rule.
  - **Three concepts that had zero prose hits** are now short sections of §1.12: one tail or two chosen from the question's wording (with the table-reading rule for each, and why picking the tail after seeing the sign lifts the real false-alarm rate from 5% to 10%), statistical versus practical significance, and absence of evidence versus evidence of absence (the two non-significant intervals that mean opposite things). "Two ways to be wrong" stopped being two bullets and became the β-and-power section the Two Curves picture belongs to; Stats 3 stays the going-deeper link.
  - **`step="0.1"` on a number input with `min="0.01"` is a trap, found in the browser and not by reading.** `SC.preset` snaps to the control's own grid with the *minimum* as the base, so that grid runs .01, .11, .21 … and a preset of `?sigma=4` landed on **4.01**, quietly moving every readout. All five free-form number inputs are `step="any"` now, which is also what lets a lecturer preset a value the slider grid could not reach. The integer sample-size fields keep `step="1"`.
  - **Parameter names are shared across the panels on a page on purpose** (`n` means sample size wherever it appears, so one link configures the whole page) and each control still clamps to its own range, so `?n=4` gives the Interval Builder 4 and the coverage playground its minimum of 5. `teachers.html` documents that, plus the two §1.12 pairings. `?sigma=` is deliberately **not** wired to Two Curves: the panel above it measures grams and that one centimeters, and one key cannot mean both.
  - **`formulas.html` gained twelve rows** an intro formula sheet carries and the site lacked: the general addition and multiplication rules, a discrete random variable's mean and variance, the binomial mean and SD, the z-interval, the margin of error, n for a stated margin, the one-sample z, the two-sample SE with the conservative-df note, and the four proportion rows. Registries: glossary gained *z-interval*, *Margin of error* and *Practical significance*; three stats-1 quiz questions; both lessons' `checks.js` trios rewritten around the new material; **Problems 17 and 18** (a blood-pressure z-interval at two levels then n for a margin of 1.5 → 28 readings, and a bulb-lifetime test where the tail and the α each flip the verdict: one-tailed p = .0401 rejects at .05 and not at .01, two-sided p = .0801 rejects at neither); and §1.12's "fail to reject" FAQ was swapped for "Why learn the z-test when σ is almost never known?", since the new "absence of evidence" section had made the old answer a reprise of the prose.
  - Gates: `audit.js` 0 errors, `math-check.js` 623/623 (viz.js untouched), `prose-lint.js --strict` green, every editorial checker clean or ACKED. Verified in a real Chromium against both `serve.js` roots: every readout above, the presets landing, garbage params ignored (and `?sigma=0` clamping to the control minimum, which is the documented behavior rather than a bug), `?embed=1` composing with the new params, the beforeprint canvas snapshot and its restore, light and dark, and no page-level horizontal scroll at 360px on the four touched pages.
